Ultra-Granular Study Guide · SO LDCE / In-Service Exams

Travelling Allowance (TA) Rules — 7th CPC

Entitlements, Daily Allowance, Mileage, Transfer TA, Retirement TA, key amendment OMs and worked illustrations. Updated 22 November 2026 with targeted corrections and explicit verification caveats; this is a study aid, not a full legal audit or an individual entitlement decision.

This study guide is based on the Department of Expenditure's O.M. No.19030/1/2017-E.IV dated 13.07.2017 and selected subsequent materials. It contains explanatory material and examples that have not all been independently verified. The update register below records targeted corrections made on 22.11.2026; it is not a line-by-line legal validation.

Provenance & coverage note. Primary source requested: YouTube playlist "Travelling Allowance Rules — TA Rules" by Just Crack It (6 parts, ~5.5 hrs; Part 1 based on DoE O.M. dated 13.07.2017, Parts 2–4 on Swamy's Handbook, Part 5 on important amendment O.Ms, Part 6 on worked illustrations). YouTube did not expose a machine-readable transcript/caption track for this series (transcript panel returned no text on repeated attempts) — so no sentence from the spoken narration is quoted here. The guide is intended to use (a) the official DoE O.M. No.19030/1/2017‑E.IV dated 13.07.2017 and selected amendment O.Ms, and (b) Dearness-Allowance-linked rate-revision materials. This provenance is not a certification that every fact card, rate, or citation has been rechecked; see the targeted correction register above. Chapter topics mirror the video series' own stated structure (from its descriptions/titles) so the guide can still be used alongside the videos. Explanatory analogies, memory hooks, and exam notes should be treated as study aids, not authority.
Current Dearness Allowance correction (updated 22.11.2026): The Department of Expenditure O.M. No. 1/2(i)/2026-E.II(B), dated 29.10.2026, revises Central Government employees' Dearness Allowance (DA) to 63% of Basic Pay w.e.f. 01.07.2026. Terminology guard: this 63% pay-linked figure is Dearness Allowance, not the Daily Allowance claimed for eligible official travel. This corrects the stale 60% / 01.01.2026 statements below. The 2024 crossing of 50% (effective 01.01.2024) is a separate trigger; 63% Dearness Allowance remains below the next stated 100% trigger. The 25%-enhanced Daily Allowance rate tables reproduced from the 2024 source are not recalculated by a 3-point change in Dearness Allowance; Dearness-Allowance-linked Transport Allowance examples are corrected below. This targeted update does not certify every rate, formula, claim, or entitlement in the guide.
Targeted correction register — 22.11.2026 (not a whole-guide verification)
  • Dearness Allowance (DA): Updated to 63% from 01.07.2026; the pay-linked figure is distinguished from tour Daily Allowance. DA-dependent disability Transport Allowance examples recalculated.
  • 16.07.2026 DoPT LTC circular: A Government-hosted DGT copy of F.No. G-14019/02/2026-Cash was retrieved. It is addressed to DoPT officers/officials and concerns LTC air booking and LTC advances only: the 21-day booking lead is encouragement, and air-entitled employees may apply for an LTC advance at least 30 days ahead with the specified printout from the authorized travel agency webpage showing suitable flight/fare details. It does not establish a general TA or rail-booking deadline. A direct DoPT-hosted copy was not obtained in this pass; the cited PDF is hosted by DGT.
  • TA advances: Removed the unsupported general 90% cap and general 30-day transfer-advance deadline; corrected the GFR 224/290 citations. The inspected 2005 Advances Compendium has not been verified as current; its 15-day tour-advance text is labelled accordingly.
  • Joining Time / TTA on competitive appointment: The 2015 Rule 4(4) amendment and DoPT's 2022 consolidation create an unresolved interaction about the three-year limitation. The categorical entitlement table, flowchart and answers are replaced/qualified. Technical-resignation status remains distinct from TTA and Joining Time Pay.
  • Training TA (SR 164): Removed the categorical second-station 180-day reset, blanket >180-day transfer wording, and Urban-Agglomeration extension. Current SR/GIO and historical order status remain unresolved; see the targeted source matrix. MCQ-TA-14 has no validated key.
  • Local-journey source update: The signed 12.09.2018 DoE letter supports the beyond-8-km-from-normal-duty-point test (regardless of journey origin) and mileage plus 50% DA. It does not settle at/below-8-km fares/vehicle treatment or the DFPR election. Full current SR 71/GIO and operative conveyance-hire instructions remain open; other unrevised material and all MCQs remain unverified.

Key source links: DoE Dearness Allowance update · G.S.R. 229(E), 27.03.2015 · DoPT Joining Time consolidation · DoPT Technical Resignation OM · GFR updated to 31.01.2026 · 2005 Advances Compendium copy reviewed (currency unverified).

⚠ Scope Disclaimer — Civilian vs Defence Personnel. This guide covers Civilian Central Government Ministries/Departments governed by the Department of Expenditure (DoE) O.M. No.19030/1/2017-E.IV. Defence Personnel (Army, Navy, Air Force) and personnel of CISF, Assam Rifles, and other Central Armed Police Forces (CAPFs) are governed by separate orders issued by the PCDA (Principal Controller of Defence Accounts), Pune — not by the civilian DoE O.M. While the broad rationale (Pay-Level-based entitlements, Dearness-Allowance-linked rate revisions, CTG structure) is similar, the specific rates, class entitlements, and Road Mileage Allowance (RMA) tables may differ in Defence orders. For competitive exams like CDS, AFCAT, or NDA that test Defence-specific TA, always refer to the latest PCDA(O) Pune circulars. This guide's RMA state-wise table (Chapter 4) is included only as an illustration of the "state-specific rate" concept — it does not constitute the governing rate for civilian employees.

This guide is substantial, so use a route instead of reading every section in one sitting. The core rule chapters come first; amendments, exceptions, appendices and AO tools are later passes. Check the source status before memorising a rule and use the links below to return to the right material quickly.

Official DoE PDF		If a Central Government employee on tour is unable to submit a boarding pass with the TA bill, the annexed self-declaration may be submitted in lieu; below-Under-Secretary/Pay Level 10-and-below cases require Controlling Officer countersignature.The text does not require an airline “Certificate of Travel” or a fee; applies to tour journeys and stated conditions.

It does not authenticate the distinct fixed-rate residential-training allowance or a universal formula for every partial/free-facility scenario.

Official DoE PDF		Ordinary TA claim period is 60 days succeeding the applicable trigger; para 2 separately reckons family journeys and delivery of personal effects/conveyance. Para 5 is a narrow investigation route for claims kept in abeyance over one year, not blanket condonation.Claim-submission clocks are not advance-adjustment deadlines.

Official DoE PDF		Hotel/guest-house entitlement is exclusive of taxes; GST is reimbursable over the prescribed entitlement for actual charges paid within that entitlement.Does not remove the underlying hotel ceiling or validate every hotel/tax claim.

Official DoE PDF		Retirement TA submission window is 180 days; tour, transfer and training remain 60 days.Retirement-specific change only; apply the proper journey trigger.

Official DoE PDF		For retirement settlement, full 80% CTG is available subject to actual change of residence; the 20-km condition is removed.Retirement rule only; do not import it into transfer CTG.

Check the exact order and relaxation for a real claim.

Route 1 · Exam study

Understand → retrieve → apply

  1. Chapters 1–2: build the rule framework and travel-entitlement map.
  2. Chapters 3–4: study tour/DA and mileage, then work the examples.
  3. Chapters 5–6: study transfer and retirement components, keeping their separate CTG and deadline rules apart.
  4. Chapters 7–8: use Chapter 7 as a selected historical amendment chronology, then study the current-rate mechanism; do not treat every old entry as current.
  5. Chapters 9–11: practise calculations, definitions, deadlines and exceptions; keep source holds visible.
  6. Chapters 12–17: use the technical-resignation, joining-time, case-study, comparison, MCQ and FAQ material as a specialist extension.
  7. Finish with Appendices A–D: use flashcards and reference indexes for retrieval; keep unverified MCQs unscored, then consult the source-check log and optional claim toolkit.

Route 2 · Claim / audit desk

Classify → verify → calculate → document

  1. Classify the journey: identify the claim type and the facts that trigger the rule.
  2. Separate the clocks: check the deadline matrix; do not merge claim-submission and advance-adjustment periods.
  3. Assemble the evidence: use the practical checklist and record missing documents or approvals.
  4. Check the arithmetic: review assumptions, inputs and calculations before preparing a bill.
  5. Resolve conflicts: use the consistency sweep and TA/LTC comparison, then confirm the controlling primary order and competent authority.

This is a navigation aid, not an authority or individual entitlement decision. A workflow cannot upgrade an unverified rule.

Suggested seven-session study plan

  1. Session 1 · Foundations: Chapters 1–2.
  2. Session 2 · Tour and road travel: Chapters 3–4.
  3. Session 3 · Transfer and retirement: Chapters 5–6.
  4. Session 4 · History and current rates: Chapters 7–8; learn the effect/scope of checked orders, not an unverified chronology by rote.
  5. Session 5 · Apply the rules: Chapters 9–11.
  6. Session 6 · Specialist material: Chapters 12–15.
  7. Session 7 · Retrieval and verification: Chapters 16–17, then Appendices A–D and the source-check log. Keep held items unscored.

How to use repeated summaries and historical material

Fact-sheets, rapid-revision recaps, flashcards and the AO quick-reference sheet intentionally repeat selected rules for recall. They are summaries, not separate authority; if a number or shorthand differs, return to the full chapter and the source-status entry. Chapter 7 is a selected historical chronology, not a current-rule list. Check issue date, effective date, scope and later amendments before using an old O.M.

Use the same loop in every chapter

  1. State the rule: identify the trigger, scope, exception and effective date.
  2. Check the source status: distinguish a primary-source finding from a secondary lead or an unresolved point.
  3. Apply it: work the illustration or case without looking at the explanation.
  4. Retrieve it: close the section, answer its questions, then check the source. Mark each point know / unsure / source check and revisit it the next day and again about a week later; never turn “unsure” into an assumed rule.

Source-status key

Status applies only to the specific proposition, authority, date and scope described in that card—not automatically to the whole chapter.

Primary source reviewedUse only within the wording, scope and effective period actually checked; look for later amendments.
Government-hosted reproduction / secondary leadUseful for locating text; a reproduction is not automatically the issuer’s signed record or proof of current/general applicability.
UNVERIFIED / HOLDDo not score or apply as a settled rule until the relevant primary authority and scope are checked.

Foundations: Legal Basis, Definitions & Kinds of TA

Very High-YieldLegal basis & supersession chain

Exact-Source Fact Card

Use this source map to separate the governing framework, rate-setting orders and claim-processing clocks:

Legal framework, source role and boundary
TopicRule / authorityEffect and boundary
TA frameworkFundamental Rules (FR) and Supplementary Rules (SR), specifically SR 17–SR 246, as revised by Department of Expenditure (DoE), Ministry of Finance Office Memoranda.The O.M.s revise and supplement the underlying rules; check the current instrument for the issue at hand.
7th CPC base orderDoE O.M. No. 19030/1/2017-E.IV dated 13.07.2017.Superseded O.M. No. 19030/3/2008-E.IV dated 23.09.2008 and applies from 01.07.2017.
Pay-Level basisCCS (Revised Pay) Rules, 2016 Pay Matrix.The Pay Level shown in that matrix determines TA/DA entitlement; the guide’s rate and entitlement tables are not all independently revalidated.
GFR roleGeneral Financial Rules (GFR) 2017; Rule 290 and DoE O.M. 13.03.2018.GFR does not separately prescribe TA rates; its role here is procedural/financial-propriety. Rule 290 and the O.M. provide the ordinary claim-submission framework.
Ordinary TA claimTour, transfer and training TA: 60 days succeeding the applicable trigger, whether or not an advance was drawn.DoE O.M. 13.03.2018 para 2 provides separate triggers for relevant family journeys and delivered personal effects/conveyances.
Retirement TA claim180 days under DoE O.M. 15.06.2021.This is a separate claim-submission clock, not an advance-adjustment period.
Claim kept in abeyanceDoE O.M. 13.03.2018 para 5.Provides a specific review route where a claim is kept in abeyance for more than one year; it is not blanket condonation.
Advance adjustmentSeparate issue from the above submission clocks.Do not infer an advance-adjustment deadline from the 60-day or 180-day claim-submission periods.

Source boundary: This is a navigation summary, not a complete validation of current SR 114, SR 164, later amendments or advance-adjustment instructions.

Simple Terms (Hinglish)
  • TA/DA ka poora system kaise chalta hai? Basically, Travelling Allowance ka system FR (Fundamental Rules) aur SR (Supplementary Rules) pe based hai — ye bohot purane rules hain jo British era se chale aa rahe hain. Inhi rules ko time-time pe Finance Ministry (Department of Expenditure) apne O.M. (Office Memorandum) ke through update karta rehta hai. To agar aapko TA/DA ka koi rule samajhna hai, to pehle FR/SR dekho, phir latest O.M. check karo.
  • 7th CPC ka "Master Order" kaunsa hai? 7th Pay Commission lagne ke baad, 13 July 2017 ko ek comprehensive O.M. nikla — No. 19030/1/2017-E.IV — jo TA Rules ka "Bible" ban gaya. Isi ek O.M. ne 6th CPC ke purane 2008 wale order (No. 19030/3/2008-E.IV, dated 23.09.2008) ko poori tarah replace (supersede) kar diya. Matlab ab 2008 wala order kisi kaam ka nahi — sirf 2017 wala O.M. aur uske baad ke amendments hi lagu hain.
  • Effective date ka confusion (Classic Trap): Ye O.M. issue to 13 July 2017 ko hua, lekin applicable 1 July 2017 se hai — yani 13 din pehle ki journey bhi isi naye rule ke hisaab se claim hogi. Exams mein ye bohot puchha jaata hai — "13.07.2017 ya 01.07.2017?" — answer hamesha 01.07.2017 (effective date) hai, issue date nahi. Socho jaise salary hike January se milta hai chahe order March mein nikle — same concept.
  • Pay Level kya hai? Grade Pay khatam kyun hua? Pehle (6th CPC mein) aapki TA/DA entitlement aapke Grade Pay (jaise ₹4200, ₹5400, ₹6600 etc.) se decide hoti thi. 7th CPC ne Grade Pay ka system poori tarah khatam kar diya aur uski jagah "Pay Level" (Level 1 se Level 18 tak) laaya. Ye Pay Level CCS (Revised Pay) Rules, 2016 ke Pay Matrix mein defined hai. Example: Agar aapka Grade Pay pehle ₹4200 tha, to ab aap Level 6 mein ho — aur aapki TA/DA entitlement Level 6 ke hisaab se hogi. Simple mapping: GP 1800→L1, GP 2800→L5, GP 4200→L6, GP 5400→L9/L10, GP 6600→L11, GP 7600→L12, GP 8700→L13, GP 10000→L14.
  • GFR aur TA Rules mein kya farq hai? (Exam Trap) Ye bohot important distinction hai. GFR (General Financial Rules) TA ke rates nahi batate — wo sirf procedure aur financial discipline batate hain. Example: ordinary Tour/Transfer/Training TA claims are due within 60 days after journey completion, whether or not an advance was drawn; retirement TA is 180 days under the later DoE O.M. dated 15.06.2021. These are submission clocks, not advance-adjustment deadlines. Lekin kitna paisa milega (kitna hotel, kitna food, kitna mileage) — ye GFR nahi batata, ye TA Rules (O.M. 13.07.2017) batate hain. Exam mein trap ye hota hai: "GFR Rule 290 prescribes ₹30/km mileage rate" — ye galat hai, GFR Rule 290 concerns ordinary TA claim submission; retirement TA and advance adjustment are separate questions.
  • Real-life example: Maan lo aap ek Section Officer (Level 7) ho Delhi mein. Aapko Mumbai 3 din ki training pe bheja gaya. Ab aapka TA claim kaise banega? (1) Aapka Pay Level 7 hai, to aap Economy class flight le sakte ho, AC-II train bhi, hotel ceiling ₹937.50/day hai, food lump sum ₹1,000/day milega. (2) Bill 60 din ke andar submit karna hai (GFR Rule 290). (3) Ye sab rules O.M. 13.07.2017 se aate hain, GFR se nahi. Bas yahi poora system hai!
Memory Hook

"2017 beats 2008, Pay Level beats Grade Pay, GFR-290 = ordinary TA claim clock."

Exam Angle / PYQ

Examiners love to test the exact O.M. number (19030/1/2017-E.IV) and date (13.07.2017), the superseded order (19030/3/2008-E.IV, 23.09.2008), and the effective date (01.07.2017 — note this is before the issue date, a classic trap). Also frequently tested: confusing the GFR 60-day general limit with the 180-day special limit for retirement TA claims (Chapter 6).

High-YieldKinds of Travelling Allowance

Exact-Source Fact Card

Four broad kinds of Travelling Allowance are recognised. Compare their purpose and main components:

Four broad TA categories
KindWhen it appliesMain elements / scope
TA on TourOfficial journey away from headquarters.Travel fare plus Daily Allowance (DA).
TA on TransferChange of headquarters.Travel fare, Composite Transfer Grant (CTG), and transportation of personal effects/conveyance, each subject to its own conditions.
TA on RetirementEligible one-way journey after retirement.Journey entitlement to the declared home town or place of settlement, subject to the applicable retirement rules.
Permanent / Fixed TA and Mileage AllowanceLocal journeys and journeys by own conveyance where no specific State rate is prescribed.Fixed-allowance or per-kilometre treatment, as applicable; do not assume a default rate where a state-specific rate applies.

Related component, not a fifth kind: DA on Tour is a lump-sum/reimbursement mechanism and a component of tour TA, covering hotel, local travel and food.

Simple Terms (Hinglish)
  • TA on Tour — Sabse common type: Jab bhi aap apne headquarters (HQ) se bahar kisi official kaam se jaate ho — chahe wo training ho, meeting ho, inspection ho, ya court mein deposition ho — to ye "Tour" kehlata hai. Isme aapko do cheezein milti hain: (1) Travel fare — yaani aane-jaane ka ticket ka paisa (train/air/bus), aur (2) Daily Allowance (DA) — yaani wahan rehne (hotel), khane (food lump sum), aur local ghoomne (auto/taxi) ka paisa. Example: Aap Delhi se Mumbai 3 din ki training pe gaye — to flight ka ticket + hotel ka paisa + khane ka lump sum + Mumbai mein auto ka paisa — sab milega. Tour tab shuru hota hai jab aap HQ chhodte ho, aur tab khatam hota hai jab aap wapas HQ pahunchte ho.
  • TA on Transfer — Jab posting badalti hai: Transfer TA ek alag journey category hai jisme self/family travel, CTG, personal effects aur conveyance jaise components ho sakte hain. Inki eligibility aur duration classification ko order, public-interest/fact pattern aur current SR 114 wording se confirm karo; “more than 180 days = transfer / otherwise tour” is guide mein verified universal rule nahi hai. DoE 13.07.2017 base rates checked hain, lekin har component/condition nahi.
  • TA on Retirement — Akhri journey ka paisa: Jab aap retire hote ho (superannuation, invalid pension, ya compensation pension pe), to aapko aur aapki poori family ko ek baar apne home town ya declared settlement place tak jaane ka TA milta hai. Isme bhi Transfer TA jaisa hi milta hai — fare + CTG + baggage + conveyance. Lekin dhyan rahe: (1) Ye sirf pension pe retire hone walon ko milta hai — resignation, dismissal ya removal pe nahi. (2) Journey 1 saal ke andar complete karni hai retirement date se. (3) Claim 180 din ke andar submit karna hai. (4) Agar settle hone ki jagah home town se door hai, to reimbursement home town tak ke fare pe cap ho jayega.
  • Mileage Allowance — Jab apni gaadi se jao: Agar aap official tour pe apni car, taxi, auto-rickshaw, ya scooter se travel karte ho, to aapko per kilometre ke hisaab se paisa milta hai. Current rates (2024-2026, DA≥50% enhanced): Car/Taxi = ₹30/km, Auto/Scooter = ₹15/km, On foot = ₹15/km. Lekin ek important rule: agar aapke state ke Transport Directorate ne koi specific rate notify kiya hai (jaise Defence mein PCDA ne state-wise rates fix kiye hain), to wo state-specific rate apply hoga — default ₹30 nahi. Aur ek aur baat: pehla kilometre "dead km" hota hai — uska paisa nahi milta (sirf own car/taxi pe apply hota hai).
  • Yaad rakhne ka formula: "T-T-R-M" — Tour (fare + DA), Transfer (fare + CTG + baggage + car), Retirement (same as Transfer but one-time), Mileage (per-km rate). Exam mein puchha jaata hai "TA ke kitne kinds hain?" — answer hamesha 4 hai.
Memory Hook

"T-T-R-M: Tour, Transfer, Retirement, Mileage — ye 4 pillars hain TA ke."

6th CPC Grade Pay → 7th CPC Pay Level Quick Mapping (for practical reference & exam traps)

Many employees and exam candidates still think in terms of the old 6th CPC "Grade Pay" system. Here is the official correspondence under the CCS (Revised Pay) Rules, 2016 Pay Matrix:

GP 2800Level 5Senior Secretariat Assistant
GP 4200Level 6Inspector / Assistant Section Officer
GP 4600Level 7Section Officer (Entry)
GP 4800Level 8Section Officer (Senior)
GP 5400 (PB-2)Level 9Under Secretary
GP 5400 (PB-3)Level 10Deputy Secretary
GP 6600Level 11Senior Time Scale / Deputy Secretary (Sr.)
GP 7600Level 12Director / Joint Secretary (Jr.)
GP 8700Level 13Director / Joint Secretary
GP 8900Level 13ADirector (Sr.) / Addl. Secretary (Jr.)
GP 10000Level 14Additional Secretary / Secretary (Jr.)

Exam Trap: Examiners may ask "What is the TA entitlement of a GP 4200 officer?" — you must map this to Level 6 first, then look up the Level 6 entitlement (Economy air, AC-II train). The concept of "Grade Pay" no longer exists in the 7th CPC TA framework.

Master Fact-Sheet — Chapter 1

Review Protocol

If you can recall the O.M. number, both dates (issue vs effective), and the 4 kinds of TA without looking — you're solid. Remember: ordinary tour/transfer/training claim submission is 60 days whether or not an advance was drawn; retirement TA is a separate 180-day window. Neither number supplies a TA-advance adjustment deadline.

Active recall: (1) What O.M. did the 13.07.2017 order supersede? (2) From which date is the 2017 O.M. effective? (3) Which rule fixes the general 60-day TA claim limit? (4) Name the 4 kinds of TA.

Rapid-Revision Recap — Chapter 1

  • Master order: O.M. 19030/1/2017-E.IV dated 13.07.2017, effective 01.07.2017.
  • Supersedes the 2008 (6th CPC) O.M. No.19030/3/2008-E.IV.
  • Entitlement basis = "Pay Level" in the 2016 Pay Matrix, not Grade Pay.
  • GFR Rule 290 concerns the ordinary tour/transfer/training claim-submission clock: 60 days, whether or not an advance was drawn. Retirement TA has a separate 180-day order; advance adjustment is separate.
  • 4 kinds of TA: Tour, Transfer, Retirement, Mileage/Fixed.
  • GFR ≠ TA rate-setting authority — common exam trap.
  • GP → Level mapping: GP 1800→L1, GP 2800→L5, GP 4200→L6, GP 5400→L10, GP 6600→L11, GP 7600→L12, GP 8700→L13, GP 8900→L13A, GP 10000→L14.
  • Scope: Civilian Ministries only — Defence/CAPF personnel governed by PCDA(Pune) orders.
Chapter 2

Travel Entitlements by Mode & Pay Level

Chapter study mapPARTIAL
FocusSelect a travel mode/class by Pay Level and keep tour entitlement separate from LTC rules.
Source reviewThe domestic entitlement table was checked; some train-specific, sea and international details still need their exact primary orders.
Study moveRebuild the Pay-Level table from memory, then check every exception against its own order.

Very High-YieldEntitlement table — Rail / Air / Steamer by Pay Level

Exact-Source Fact Card

Table A of the O.M. dated 13.07.2017 fixes travel entitlement by 5 pay-level bands. Level 13A (Grade Pay ₹8900, added later) was clarified vide O.M. of even number dated 04.09.2017 to carry the same entitlement as Level 13.

Pay LevelTravel by AirTravel by Train
14 and aboveBusiness/Club classAC-I
12 & 13 (incl. 13A)Economy classAC-I
9 to 11Economy classAC-II
6 to 8Economy classAC-II
5 and belowNot entitled (except specific relaxations)AC-III / AC Chair Car
RED-FLAG TRAP

Flag: "Levels 6–8 are too junior for air travel." Fix: Under the 7th CPC rules, Levels 6 to 11 are entitled to Economy Class air travel on tour — a major liberalisation from the 6th CPC regime. Fix-phrase: "6 through 13 all fly economy or better; only 5-and-below stays on the ground (by air)."

Simple Terms (Hinglish)
  • Level 14 aur usse upar (Secretary / Addl. Secretary level): Ye sabse senior officers hain — inko flight mein Business Class ya Club Class milti hai (sabse mehngi class), aur train mein AC First Class (AC-I). Matlab agar ye Delhi se Mumbai ja rahe hain to Business Class ka ticket fully reimburse hoga — koi ceiling nahi hai flight fare pe. Train mein poora private cabin milta hai AC-I mein. Ye level typically Secretary, Additional Secretary, ya equivalent rank ke officers ka hota hai (basic pay ₹2,25,000+).
  • Level 12, 13 aur 13A (Director / Joint Secretary level): Inko flight mein Economy Class milti hai (Business Class nahi — ye Level 14+ ke liye reserved hai), aur train mein AC First Class (AC-I). 7th CPC se pehle (6th CPC mein) Level 13A ka koi separate mention nahi tha TA table mein — 4 September 2017 ko ek alag O.M. nikla jisne clear kiya ki Level 13A = Level 13 same entitlements. Ye bohot commonly tested point hai exams mein. Note: Level 13A ka Grade Pay pehle ₹8,900 tha — ye Director (Senior) ya equivalent posts ke liye hai.
  • Level 9 se 11 (Under Secretary / Deputy Secretary level): 7th CPC ka sabse bada gift — in levels ko pehli baar flight mein travel karne ka right mila! Pehle (6th CPC mein) sirf Level 12+ hi flight le sakte the. Ab Level 9 se 11 wale bhi Economy Class flight le sakte hain official tour pe. Train mein AC-II Tier milti hai. Example: Agar aap Under Secretary (Level 11) ho aur Delhi se Guwahati jaana hai training ke liye, to aap Indigo/Air India ki Economy class flight le sakte ho — poora paisa reimburse hoga. Hotel ceiling (current): ₹2,812.50/day, Food lump sum: ₹1,125/day.
  • Level 6 se 8 (Section Officer / Inspector level): Ye bhi Economy Class flight + AC-II train ke entitled hain — bilkul Level 9-11 jaisa! Ye 7th CPC ka revolutionary change tha. Pehle (6th CPC mein) Level 6-8 walon ko flight bilkul nahi milti thi — sirf train se travel karna padta tha. Ab ek Inspector (Level 6) bhi official tour pe flight le sakta hai! LEKIN EK BADA EXCEPTION: Ye flight entitlement sirf official tour/transfer/training pe hai. LTC (Leave Travel Concession) pe Level 6-8 ko flight nahi milti — sirf AC-II Tier train milegi (special dispensation ko chhod ke — jaise J&K, NER, Ladakh, A&N jaane ke liye). Ye sabse commonly tested trap hai!
  • Level 5 aur usse neeche (LDC, MTS, Peon etc.): Inko flight nahi milti (normally). Train mein AC-III Tier ya AC Chair Car milta hai. Koi special relaxation se (jaise medical emergency ya extreme urgency) flight mil sakti hai, lekin ye rare hai aur competent authority ki specific approval chahiye. Inka hotel ceiling (current) ₹562.50/day hai, food lump sum ₹625/day hai — ye amounts DA≥50% enhanced hain (pehle ₹450 aur ₹500 the).
  • Real-life comparison: Socho ek office mein 4 log hain — Secretary (L14), Director (L13), Section Officer (L7), aur Peon (L1). Sabko Delhi-Chennai tour pe bheja. Secretary Business Class flight lega, Director Economy flight lega, Section Officer bhi Economy flight lega (7th CPC ka gift!), aur Peon train se AC-III mein jayega. Sabka hotel bhi alag level ka hoga — Secretary ₹9,375/day tak, Director ₹5,625/day tak, SO ₹937.50/day tak, Peon ₹562.50/day tak.

High-YieldPremium / Special trains & international travel

Exact-Source Fact Card

Officials may travel by Premium Trains/Premium Tatkal Trains/Suvidha Trains, with reimbursement of Premium Tatkal charges. Dynamic/Flexi-fare in Shatabdi/Rajdhani/Duronto trains is reimbursable while on official tour/training. Tatkal Seva Charges (fixed fare) continue to be allowed. The 12.09.2022 Tejas O.M. allows Tejas travel for tour, training, transfer and retirement, with class entitlement as for Shatabdi trains. For international air travel, the entitlement cited here is based on SR 48-B and remains outside this targeted source check; confirm the operative primary text before use.

Train typePay Level entitlement
Rajdhani / Shatabdi / Duronto / Tejas (dynamic-fare)All eligible levels, as per their base class entitlement (12 & above → Executive/AC-I; 6–11 → AC-II/Chair Car; 5 & below → AC-III/Chair Car)
Analogy

Think of "dynamic fare" trains like Uber's surge pricing — the government doesn't cap your reimbursement to the "normal" fare; it reimburses whatever the surge price was, since you had no choice on an official tour.

Exam Angle / PYQ

Trap: Tatkal Seva Charge (fixed, small fee) vs Premium Tatkal (dynamic, can be large) — both are reimbursable, but examiners test whether you know both exist and are treated alike (reimbursable), unlike ordinary "convenience fee" add-ons which are not.

Master Fact-Sheet — Chapter 2

ItemValue/LimitReference
Level 14+ air/railBusiness/Club; AC-ITable A, O.M. 13.07.2017
Level 12–13 (13A) air/railEconomy; AC-ITable A + O.M. 04.09.2017 (13A)
Level 6–11 air/railEconomy; AC-IITable A
Level 5 & belowNo air; AC-III/Chair carTable A
Tejas Express clarificationAllowed on official tourO.M. 19030/1/2017-E.IV, 12.09.2022

Review Protocol

Active recall: (1) Which levels first get air-eligibility under 7th CPC? (2) What class does Level 13A get, and why? (3) Name 3 premium train categories whose dynamic fare is reimbursable.

Rapid-Revision Recap — Chapter 2

  • 5 pay-band structure for travel class; Level 13A = same as Level 13 (O.M. 04.09.2017).
  • Levels 6–11: Economy air + AC-II train — the 7th CPC's biggest liberalisation.
  • Level 5 & below: no air, AC-III/Chair car only.
  • Dynamic fare (Rajdhani/Shatabdi/Duronto) + Tatkal + Tejas (2022) all reimbursable.
  • International air scale (SR 48-B): 17+ First, 14-16 Business, ≤13 Economy.
Chapter 3

TA on Tour & Daily Allowance

Chapter study mapPARTIAL / OPEN
FocusSeparate fare, hotel/local-travel and food Daily Allowance; identify time bands, facilities and local-journey rules.
Source reviewBase DA structure/current input has targeted coverage, but voucher conditions are partly pending; local-journey/GIO sources and SR 164 training claims remain open.
Study moveFor each example, state the journey type, time band and facility facts; keep SR 164 schedules/options on hold.

Very High-YieldThe lump-sum DA system (replacing old reimbursement)

Exact-Source Fact Card

Tour Daily Allowance is divided into three components. The table separates the payment method from the documentation conditions:

Tour DA components and documentation
ComponentPayment methodDocumentation / scope note
Hotel / staying accommodationReimbursement of eligible charges up to the applicable ceiling.For Levels 8 and below, the 13.07.2017 O.M. provides a self-certified route up to the ceiling. It separately states a Class-X ceiling of ₹1,000/day for these levels against relevant vouchers. The interaction for a Class-X claim within the ordinary ceiling remains unresolved in this guide.
Local travelling chargesReimbursement up to the applicable Pay-Level ceiling.Levels 8 and below may use the stated self-certification route. A separate 22.12.2020 O.M. covers Level 9–11 local-travel documentation under its conditions; it does not itself change vehicle entitlement or ceiling.
FoodLump sum; it replaced the earlier food-bill reimbursement approach.No separate food vouchers are required under the cited 2017 O.M.

Class-X caution: Do not state that every Level 8-and-below Class-X hotel claim necessarily requires a voucher—or that none does—based on this summary. The ₹1,000/day Class-X ceiling is voucher-backed, but the overlap with the general self-certification route within the ordinary ceiling is unresolved; MCQ-TA-06 is therefore unscored.

Simple Terms (Hinglish)
  • DA ka naya system — 3 alag buckets: Pehle (5th/6th CPC mein) "Daily Allowance" ek single flat number hota tha. 7th CPC ne isko teen parts mein divide kiya:
    • Hotel stay: Room-charge reimbursement, up to the applicable ceiling.
    • Local travel: Within-city travel reimbursement, up to the applicable ceiling.
    • Food: Flat lump sum; no separate food bill/voucher is required.
    Example rates for Level 9 shown in this guide: Hotel ≤₹2,812.50/day, Local Travel ≤₹422.50/day, Food ₹1,125/day. Check the applicable rate and conditions for the claim period.
  • Food ka voucher kyun nahi chahiye? Kyunki food ab ek fixed lump sum hai — jaise Railway mein aapko meal ka fixed rate milta hai, chahe aap khayein ya na khayein. Sarkar ka logic hai: "Khana to aapko khana hi hai, iska bill collect karne mein time waste hota hai." To agar aap Level 9 ho, to aapko ₹1,125/day food ka flat mil jayega — chahe aapne ₹200 ka khana khaya ya ₹2,000 ka. Ye aapka personal paisa hai.
  • Self-certification — Level 8 aur neeche: The 13.07.2017 O.M. allows self-certified hotel/local-travel claims up to the applicable ceiling, subject to its stated details. For Class-X cities, the O.M. separately sets a ₹1,000/day hotel ceiling for Levels 8 and below on production of relevant vouchers. Whether that voucher condition also applies to claims within the ordinary ceiling is unresolved here; do not treat either reading as settled.
  • Level 9+ walon ke liye kya rule hai? 22 December 2020 ke O.M. ne ek aur relaxation di: ab Level 9 se 11 wale bhi local city travel ka claim bina voucher ke self-certificate pe kar sakte hain (apni ceiling ke andar). Pehle sirf Level 8 aur neeche walon ko ye facility thi. Ab sirf Level 12 aur usse upar walon ko local travel ke vouchers dena zaroori hai.
  • Teen "envelopes" ka concept: Socho DA ka paisa 3 alag envelopes mein aata hai — ek pe "HOTEL" likha hai, ek pe "LOCAL TAXI," ek pe "FOOD." Har envelope ki apni ceiling hai. Agar aap Hotel mein kam kharcha kiya (ceiling se neeche), to bache hue paise ko aap Food ya Travel mein transfer nahi kar sakte. Har envelope independent hai — jo bacha wo gaya!
  • Class-X scope caution: The ₹1,000/day Class-X hotel ceiling for Levels 8 and below is voucher-backed. The interaction with the ordinary self-certification route for claims within the ordinary ceiling remains unresolved; do not present a universal exception as settled.
Memory Hook

"H-T-F — Hotel, Travel, Food: three components; food is a lump sum; keep the Class-X voucher ceiling separate from the unresolved self-certification overlap."

Very High-YieldDaily Allowance on Tour — Disturbances, Curfews, Sundays, and Holiday Rules (SR 51, SR 72, SR 73)

Exact-Source Fact Card

Under Swamy's Compilation of FR & SR (Part-II, Travelling Allowances) and GIOs under SR 51, SR 72, and SR 73, the admissibility of Daily Allowance (DA) during extraordinary situations and halts on tour is strictly regulated:

  • 1. DA during Disturbances/Imposition of Curfew (GIO (4)(b), SR 51):
    If an employee's stay on tour is involuntarily extended due to natural disasters, civil disturbances, blockades, or the imposition of a curfew, and their absence is regularized as Special Casual Leave (SCL), Daily Allowance (Food & Hotel) is fully admissible for the entire period of such Special Casual Leave.
  • 2. DA for Sundays and Holidays (GIO (1), SR 72):
    Daily Allowance is admissible for Sundays and public holidays falling during a tour ONLY if the employee is actually on camp (at the tour station) on those days and not merely constructively on camp. No DA is allowed if the employee leaves the tour station to visit their family or headquarters on weekends.
  • 3. DA during Leave/Restricted Holidays (GIO (1), SR 72):
    NO Daily Allowance is admissible for any kind of leave (Casual Leave, Earned Leave, etc.) or Restricted Holidays (RH) availed of by the employee while on tour.
  • 4. The 180-Day Continuous Halt Limit (GIO (1), SR 73):
    A 180-day continuous-halt limit appears in the referenced secondary material, but the current primary text and its application to a particular training course have not been verified. Do not use this general tour note to decide training DA; see the source-status card and check the current SR/GIO and facility terms.
Simple Terms (Hinglish)
  • 1. Curfew / Danga / Natural Disaster mein phas gaye to kya? Socho aap Srinagar tour pe gaye ho aur achanak curfew lag gaya ya floods aa gaye — aap wahan phas gaye. Ab aap chahte to nahi ruk rahe the, lekin majboori mein rukhna pada. Aise cases mein aapki ye extra rukne ki duration Special Casual Leave (SCL) maani jaati hai — aur is poore SCL period ka DA (Hotel + Food dono) fully milta hai. Matlab agar 5 din curfew raha, to 5 din ka poora DA milega — koi cutting nahi. Lekin condition ye hai ki ye rukna involuntary hona chahiye — agar aap apni marzi se ruk gaye "ghumne ke liye" to ye rule nahi lagega.
  • 2. Sunday ya Holiday tour pe pad jaaye to? Agar aap tour pe ho aur beech mein Sunday ya koi sarkari holiday aa jaaye, to us din ka DA tabhi milega jab aap sach mein us tour station pe physically maujood ho ("on camp" ho). Example: Aap Jaipur tour pe ho Monday se Friday tak, aur Saturday-Sunday bhi Jaipur mein ruke rahe — to Saturday-Sunday ka bhi DA milega. Lekin agar aap Saturday ko ghar (Delhi) aa gaye aur Monday ko wapas Jaipur gaye — to Saturday-Sunday ka DA nahi milega, kyunki aap "on camp" nahi the. Ye "constructive vs actual presence" ka concept exams mein bohot test hota hai.
  • 3. Tour ke beech chhutti le li to? Agar aap tour pe ho aur beech mein Casual Leave (CL), Earned Leave (EL), ya Restricted Holiday (RH) le liya, to us chhutti wale din ka DA poora kat jayega — NIL. Example: Aap 5 din ke tour pe ho, lekin Day 3 pe aapne CL le li — to Day 3 ka DA zero. Sirf baaki 4 din ka DA milega. Reason: DA sirf "duty" ke din ke liye milta hai — leave pe DA nahi mil sakta.
  • 4. Training and the 180-day duration: Secondary transcriptions refer to 180-day training schedules, but the current SR 164/GIO text and their scope have not been verified. DoE O.M. 01.02.2018 separately addresses free boarding/lodging. Do not infer a current universal training ceiling or station reset from this study aid; see Chapter 11.

Very High-YieldLocal-Journey TA & DA — SR 71 Clarification and Source Status

Targeted signed-source check · 12.09.2018

The signed Department of Expenditure letter No. 904302/2018-E.IV dated 12.09.2018 (scan of the signed letter, hosted by a secondary site) says local-journey TA is admissible when the temporary duty point is beyond 8 km from the normal place of duty, whether the journey is made from residence or from the normal place of duty. For that journey it states mileage allowance plus 50% of DA. The letter does not reproduce current SR 71/GIO text and does not settle journeys at/below 8 km, general public-fare/private-vehicle rules, or a DFPR election. A separate official DoE O.M. dated 22.01.2009 allowed a historical complete-package choice between the 23.09.2008 and 17.04.1998 daily-allowance-on-tour schedules for a particular tour; it concerns tour DA, not local journeys or DFPR conveyance-hire, and its continuing status was not traced here. The DoE 13.07.2017 O.M. states that it supersedes the 23.09.2008 O.M. in respect of TA. The official DFPR 2024 Gazette, Annexure-II (see Rule 12), “General Conditions for incurring Expenditures,” para (8), only refers conveyance-hire financial limits/guidelines to extant Finance Ministry instructions; it sets no local-journey threshold, rate, election, or DA consequence.

  • 1. Local-journey DA components in the signed clarification:
    For the qualifying journey beyond 8 km from the normal duty point, hotel accommodation is not applicable. The letter sets local food DA at 50% of the tour food lump sum by absence band. The 13.07.2017 O.M. reckons absence midnight-to-midnight:
    Length of AbsenceLocal Journey Food DA Rate
    Less than 6 hours (< 6 hrs)15% of the tour food lump sum (50% of the 30% tour band)
    Between 6 to 12 hours (6–12 hrs)35% of the tour food lump sum (50% of the 70% tour band)
    More than 12 hours (> 12 hrs)50% of the tour food lump sum (50% of the 100% tour band)
  • 2. Mileage / conveyance treatment — verified and open issues:
    The signed 12.09.2018 letter states mileage allowance plus 50% of DA for the qualifying local journey.
    • Not stated in that letter: a DFPR alternative, binding election, or “no DA” consequence.
    • DFPR 2024 Annexure-II, para. (8): refers conveyance-hire financial limits/guidelines to extant Finance Ministry instructions; the operative instruction has not been verified.
  • 3. Journeys at or below 8 km — treatment not established:
    The signed letter measures the qualifying distance from the normal place of duty, even when travel starts at residence; it does not decide trips at or below 8 km.
    • No reviewed primary text here establishes a general public-conveyance-fare entitlement for those trips.
    • No reviewed primary text here establishes a blanket taxi/auto/own-car ban or “no reimbursement of any kind.”
Simple Terms (Hinglish)
  • "Local Journey" kya hai? Signed 12.09.2018 DoE letter ke mutabik, temporary duty point normal place of duty se 8 km se zyada ho to local-journey TA admissible hai — chahe aap residence se jao ya normal duty point se. Current SR 71/GIO ka full text aur municipal/UA boundaries abhi independently verify nahi hue.
  • Local-journey Food DA (sirf qualifying beyond-8-km cases): Signed letter mein hotel accommodation “not applicable” hai; food component tour amount ka 50% hai:
    • <6 hours: 15% of tour food lump sum (Level 9 illustration: ₹168.75 if ₹1,125 applies).
    • 6–12 hours: 35% of tour food lump sum (Level 9 illustration: ₹393.75 if ₹1,125 applies).
    • >12 hours: 50% of tour food lump sum (Level 9 illustration: ₹562.50 if ₹1,125 applies).
    Ye signed letter ke figures hain; complete current SR 71/GIO aur under-threshold treatment abhi open hai.
  • Mileage aur DFPR: Qualifying local journey ke liye signed letter mileage allowance + 50% DA kehta hai. DFPR alternative, binding election, aur “no DA” condition primary operative instructions se verify nahi hue; DFPR 2024 Gazette Annexure-II(8) sirf extant Finance Ministry instructions ko refer karta hai.
  • ⚠ 8 km test: Residence se distance ko threshold mat samjho — signed letter test ko normal place of duty se measure karta, journey chahe residence se shuru ho. At/below 8 km public fare, taxi/auto ya own-car treatment yahan establish nahi hai; “zero reimbursement” ya blanket public-fare rule mat likho.

Fact/Date/Number HeavyCurrently Payable Daily Allowance Rates — Hotel / Local Travel / Food lump sum, by Pay Level

The current DA is 63% w.e.f. 01.07.2026; that fact alone does not recalculate these fixed ceilings. Analogy

Think of these three ceilings like a travel per-diem "wallet" split into three labelled envelopes — Hotel, Local-Taxi, Food — each with its own cap; you can't overspend one envelope by underspending another.

High-YieldTiming rules — how much DA for how long you were away

Exact-Source Fact Card

Absence from headquarters is reckoned on a midnight-to-midnight (calendar day) basis under the 13.07.2017 O.M. The tour food-DA scale is <6 hours: 30%; 6–12 hours: 70%; >12 hours: 100%. The signed DoE letter No. 904302/2018-E.IV dated 12.09.2018 states that, for qualifying local journeys (temporary duty point beyond 8 km from normal place of duty), local food DA is 50% of the tour amount: 15% / 35% / 50%. The complete current SR 71/GIO and at/below-8-km treatment remain unverified.

Length of absence from HQ% of lump-sum DA payable
Less than 6 hours30%
6 to 12 hours70%
More than 12 hours100%
Trap note: keep the 30/70/100% tour-food-DA scale separate from the signed 12.09.2018 DoE local-journey clarification, which states 15/35/50% of the tour amount for journeys beyond 8 km from the normal place of duty. The full current SR 71/GIO text remains open.
Worked Example

Scenario: An officer in Level 9 tours from 9:00 AM to 4:00 PM the same day (7 hours), headquartered in a non-X city.
Given: Level 9 lump-sum food DA = ₹1,125/day (current rate).
Step 1: Absence duration = 7 hours → falls in the "6–12 hours" band.
Step 2: Applicable percentage = 70%.
Step 3: DA payable = 70% × ₹1,125 = ₹787.50.
Result: ₹787.50 is the food-component lump sum; hotel/travel components are claimed separately if actually incurred.
Counter-example: If the same officer's tour lasted 5 hours 45 minutes instead, they'd drop into the "<6 hours" band and receive only 30% (₹337.50) — a 40-percentage-point cliff for a 15-minute difference. This cliff-edge is a favourite trap in exams.

Use this as a warning, not as a ready-to-calculate schedule. DoE O.M. The current official SR 164/GIO text and its order history were not retrieved. Historical/secondary transcriptions describe different training scenarios; they are research leads, not proof that a schedule remains operative for every course.

No boarding/lodging providedSecondary SR 164 transcription: full DA for the first 180 days and nil thereafter. Current primary text/status not verified.
Institute has boarding/lodging facilitiesSecondary SR 164 transcription: first 30 days full DA and next 150 days half DA. Do not relabel this as the free-facilities rule.

Before calculating: distinguish free facilities from compulsory facilities charged at fixed rates; verify the current rule/order, programme sanction, trainee status, and actual costs. See workspace file TA_Rules_Source_Matrix_Training_SR164.csv.

High-YieldSplit Duty & Combined Absence — DA Calculation Rule

Exact-Source Fact Card

Under the TA Rules, Daily Allowance is calculated on a calendar-day (midnight-to-midnight) basis. This means that if an official is required to leave headquarters for a task, return mid-day, and then go out again the same day (a "split duty" scenario), the total absence from headquarters within that single calendar day is aggregated for DA calculation:

  • Split Duty Example: An official leaves HQ at 9:00 AM, returns at 1:00 PM (4 hours absent), then goes out again at 3:00 PM and returns at 7:00 PM (4 hours absent). Total absence on that calendar day = 4 + 4 = 8 hours. This falls in the 6–12 hours band → 70% DA is payable.
  • Key Principle: The periods are not treated as two separate journeys each attracting 30% DA. Instead, the combined total absence determines the applicable percentage band for the entire day.
  • Local-journey scope: The signed 12.09.2018 letter gives the 15/35/50 food-DA scale for qualifying local journeys (temporary duty point beyond 8 km from normal place of duty). It does not address split-trip aggregation; do not extend the ordinary-tour aggregation statement to local journeys without the controlling current SR/GIO.
Simple Terms (Hinglish)
  • Split Duty kya hoti hai? Imagine karo aapka office Delhi mein hai. Subah 9 baje aapko Noida (25 km door) jaana pada kisi meeting ke liye. Dopahar 1 baje wapas aa gaye (4 ghante gaye). Phir shaam ko 3 baje dobara Gurugram (30 km door) jaana pada doosri meeting ke liye. Shaam 7 baje wapas aa gaye (phir se 4 ghante gaye). Ab sawal ye hai: DA kaise calculate hoga — dono trips ka alag-alag, ya ek saath?
  • Answer: Total jodo, phir band dekho! Rule ye hai ki ek calendar day (midnight se midnight) mein jitne bhi absences hain, unhe sabko add karo: 4 hours + 4 hours = 8 hours total absence. Ab 8 hours kaunse band mein aata hai? → 6-12 hours band → 70% DA. To Level 9 officer ko (food DA ₹1,125) → 70% × ₹1,125 = ₹787.50 milega us din ka.
  • Agar alag-alag treat karte to kya hota? Agar har trip ko alag treat karte: 4 hours = <6 hours band = 30% DA, phir 4 hours = <6 hours band = 30% DA. Total = 30% + 30% = 60%. Lekin rule combined calculation karta hai → 70% milta hai. So combined method employee ke favor mein hai — 10% zyada DA milta hai is example mein.
  • Local-journey exception: Signed DoE letter 12.09.2018 gives 15/35/50 Food DA for qualifying local journeys. It does not address split-trip aggregation, so the local exception in this split-duty example remains unresolved; confirm the current SR/GIO before applying it.
  • Practical tip: Apne TA bill mein split duty ke case mein total absence clearly mention karo — "Total absence on [date] = X hours" — taaki Accounts section sahi band mein calculate kare. Dono trips ke times separately likho lekin total bhi dikhao.

Transit DA for Overnight Journeys — Calendar-Day Calculation

When a train or flight spans across midnight, the DA for each calendar day is calculated separately based on the actual hours of absence on that specific day. This is not a "full day" just because you were travelling.

Worked Example: A Level 9 officer (Food DA ceiling: ₹1,125/day) boards a train departing at 8:00 AM on Day 1 and arrives at the destination at 2:00 AM on Day 2.

• Day 1: Absence from 8:00 AM to midnight (24:00) = 16 hours → >12 hours band → 100% DA = ₹1,125.
• Day 2: Absence from midnight (00:00) to 2:00 AM = 2 hours → <6 hours band → 30% DA = ₹337.50.
• Total DA for this transit: ₹1,125 + ₹337.50 = ₹1,462.50.

Key Insight: Day 2's absence is only 2 hours (midnight to 2 AM), NOT a "full travel day." Many candidates wrongly claim 100% DA for both days — a classic trap!

Duty Pass vs TA — Railway Employees' Special Rule

Indian Railway employees are entitled to Duty Passes (free rail travel on official duty) issued under Railway Board rules. This is a separate entitlement from the Travelling Allowance (TA) system covered in this guide.

• If a Railway employee uses a Duty Pass for a journey, they cannot simultaneously claim TA (travel fare) for that same leg of the journey.
• If a Railway employee is eligible for a Duty Pass but does not use it (e.g., travels by air because rail is not feasible), they cannot claim TA for that leg unless a specific relaxation is granted by the competent authority.
• Non-Railway Ministries: Employees of all other Central Government Ministries/Departments have no Duty Pass system — they claim TA as per the rules in this guide.

Exam Trap: "A Railway employee eligible for a Duty Pass chooses to travel by air on personal expense and claims TA." → Not admissible without specific relaxation.

Master Fact-Sheet — Chapter 3

Review Protocol

If shaky on the 30/70/100% scale, drill the worked example again with a different pay level. Active recall: (1) Which DA component never needs a voucher, and why? (2) At what absence duration do you cross from 30% to 70%? (3) Which official order addresses free boarding/lodging, and what remains unresolved for fixed-rate residential training? (4) In a split duty (4h + 4h same day), what DA band applies? (5) Can a Railway employee claim TA if eligible for a Duty Pass?

Rapid-Revision Recap — Chapter 3

  • DA = Hotel + Travel-within-city + Food(lump sum, no voucher needed).
  • Level ≤8: self-certification applies up to the stated ceiling; the separate Class-X ₹1,000/day hotel voucher ceiling is not enough to settle the overlap for claims within the ordinary ceiling.
  • Timing: <6h→30%, 6–12h→70%, >12h→100% of lump sum.
  • Training DA: separate absent, free, and compulsory fixed-rate facilities. The SR 164 schedules remain secondary-source leads; DoE O.M. 01.02.2018 governs its stated free-facility scenario. Verify the current order before calculating.
  • DA generally payable for max 180 days of continuous absence.
  • Absence measured midnight-to-midnight (calendar-day basis).
  • Split Duty: Combined absence in a single day determines DA band (e.g., 4h + 4h = 8h → 70% DA).
  • Overnight transit: Each calendar day's DA calculated separately (midnight to midnight).
  • Duty Pass (Railways): If eligible for Duty Pass, cannot claim TA for that leg without relaxation.
Chapter 4

Mileage Allowance for Road Journeys

Chapter study mapPARTIAL / OPEN
FocusIdentify the governing road-mileage rate and conditions for each vehicle, route and jurisdiction.
Source reviewThe 2017 base rates were checked for the audit; the dead-kilometre authority and some applications remain open. The Defence RMA example is outside civilian scope and was flagged inconsistent.
Study moveUse the applicable civilian/State rate source; do not memorize the Defence illustration as a civilian rule.

Very High-YieldOwn car/taxi/scooter — default rates where no state rate exists

Exact-Source Fact Card

At places where no specific rate has been prescribed either by the Directorate of Transport of the concerned State or of a neighbouring State, Mileage Allowance for road journeys by own car/taxi/auto-rickshaw/own scooter is payable at currently applicable (25%-enhanced) rates of ₹30 per km (own car/taxi) and ₹15 per km (auto/own scooter etc.). Where a state-specific Road Mileage Allowance (RMA) rate does exist (e.g., defence services use PCDA-notified state-wise RMA rates), that state rate governs instead.

ModeCurrent Rate (Applicable Today)
Own car / taxi₹30 per km
Auto-rickshaw / own scooter₹15 per km
Journey on foot₹15 per km (additional allowance)
Trap note: "on foot" and "auto/scooter" share the same default rate (₹15/km) — easy to mix up in MCQs, but they are conceptually different entitlements (one is a mode of local travel reimbursement, the other a foot-travel-specific add-on).

The "First Kilometre" Rule — Dead Kilometre for Road Mileage

Under the FR/SR rules (carry-over from the 6th CPC regime, still applicable in most departments), no mileage is payable for the first kilometre of a road journey performed by own car or hired taxi. The calculation of Mileage Allowance begins from the 2nd kilometre onward.

Worked Example: An officer travels 10 km by own car for an official tour.
• Billable distance: 10 − 1 = 9 km (the first km is a "dead kilometre").
• Mileage Allowance: 9 km × ₹30/km = ₹270 (NOT ₹300).
• Trap: If the question says "10 km by own car at ₹30/km" — don't blindly compute 10 × 30 = ₹300. Deduct the dead kilometre first!

Exception: This rule applies to own car and hired taxi journeys. For journeys by auto-rickshaw (metered fare) or public bus, the actual fare paid is reimbursed without this deduction.

Exam Note: While the Department of Expenditure's 2017 O.M. does not explicitly restate the dead-km rule in its main text, it continues to be applied by most Ministries' Accounts sections as an inherited FR/SR practice. Verify with your Ministry's internal TA manual for departmental applicability.

Moderate-YieldState-wise Road Mileage Allowance (RMA) — illustrative defence-services example

Exact-Source Fact Card

Where the concerned State Directorate of Transport has notified a rate, that state figure is used instead of the ₹30/₹15 default. Illustrative example — PCDA(O) Pune Circular No.1 of 2024 dated 25.01.2024 fixed Road Mileage Allowance for Officers at state-specific rates ranging from ₹15/km (Punjab) to ₹30/km (several states incl. Andhra Pradesh, Assam, Bihar, Jharkhand, Manipur, Mizoram, Nagaland, Sikkim, Tamil Nadu), with intermediate rates such as Delhi ₹27.50/km, Karnataka ₹28.75/km, Maharashtra ₹25/km, Kerala ₹25/km, Telangana ₹21.25/km, Tripura ₹18.75/km (all adjusted w.e.f. 01.01.2024).

State (sample)RMA (₹/km)State (sample)RMA (₹/km)

[Editor's note] This RMA table is a defence-accounts illustration of the "state-specific rate" concept, not itself part of the core civilian 2017 O.M. — included to make the "no specific state rate" clause concrete.
Worked Example

Scenario: A Level 6 officer travels 40 km by own car in a state with no Directorate-of-Transport-notified rate, on official tour.
Given: The current road mileage rate is ₹30/km.
Step 1: Identify applicable rate — no state rate exists, so use the default.
Step 2: Distance × rate = 40 × ₹30 = ₹1,200.
Result: ₹1,200 payable as Mileage Allowance for that road journey.
Counter-example: If this journey were in Punjab (state RMA ₹15/km, illustrative defence table), the state-specific rate would override the default — always check for a state rate first.

Rapid-Revision Recap — Chapter 4

  • Default (no state rate): ₹30/km car/taxi; ₹15/km auto/scooter; ₹15/km on foot.
  • State/neighbouring-state Directorate-of-Transport rate, if it exists, always overrides the default.
  • Defence RMA illustrates the state-rate concept: ranges ₹15–30/km depending on state (2024 circular).
  • Dead Kilometre: First km is NOT payable for own car/taxi. Calculation begins from the 2nd km.
Chapter 5

TA on Transfer

Chapter study mapPARTIAL / OPEN
FocusClassify tour versus transfer and separate CTG, baggage, conveyance and family components.
Source reviewThe 2017 CTG base and 06.01.2022 retirement CTG change were checked; personal-effects, vehicle and family-scaling conditions remain pending.
Study moveWrite the transfer trigger, residence facts and each component separately before calculating.

Very High-YieldComposite Transfer Grant (CTG)

Exact-Source Fact Card

Composite Transfer Grant is payable at 80% of the last month's basic pay for an eligible transfer involving a change of station located 20 km or more apart. For a transfer below 20 km or within the same city/station, the 13.07.2017 O.M. allows one-third of the normal CTG only if an actual change of residence is involved; otherwise CTG is nil. For transfer to/from the Island territories of Andaman & Nicobar and Lakshadweep, CTG is paid at the enhanced rate of 100% of last month's basic pay. Where both spouses (both Central Govt servants) are transferred from the same station to the same station within 6 months — but the second transfer is after 60 days of the first — full CTG is paid to the spouse transferred earlier and 50% CTG to the spouse transferred later. CTG on retirement was separately clarified admissible vide O.M. dated 06.01.2022.

ScenarioCTG rate
Transfer ≥ 20 km, general case80% of last month's basic pay
Transfer < 20 km / same cityOne-third of normal CTG if an actual residence change occurs; otherwise nil
Transfer to/from A&N / Lakshadweep100% of last month's basic pay
Both spouses transferred, same O-D, within 6 months (2nd after 60 days)Full CTG to earlier-transferred spouse; 50% to later-transferred spouse
RED-FLAG TRAP

Flag: "CTG is always 80% regardless of distance." Fix: For an eligible transfer at 20 km or more, ordinary CTG is 80%; below 20 km or within the same city/station, one-third of normal CTG applies only if an actual residence change occurs, otherwise CTG is nil. Specified A&N/Lakshadweep transfers have the 100% rate. Fix-phrase: "Below 20 km: residence change matters; the islands have a separate 100% rate."

Very High-Yield — source status openTransfer vs Tour — apply the order and current SR 114 conditions

Primary-source boundary

Transfer TA and tour TA are distinct categories, but a duration-only “180-day switch” is not established by the primary text retrieved here. The official DoE O.M. dated 13.07.2017 says the general admissibility conditions prescribed in SR 114 continue to apply to transfer TA; it does not reproduce SR 114. The current complete Central SR 114 text, its applicable orders/amendments, and the exact transfer-order conditions remain unverified in this review.

  • When a transfer order is involved: Read its purpose, stated temporary/permanent nature and period, any extension/revision, station and residence facts, and the operative TA rule before deciding which components are admissible. If classed as a transfer, fare, CTG, personal-effects and conveyance components each retain their own conditions.
  • When duty is described as temporary or a tour: Do not infer the full TA package—or the absence of CTG/personal-effects transport—solely from the number of days. Apply the current rule and the order that actually authorized the movement.
  • Training is a separate question: Secondary transcriptions report training-specific options and schedules, but current SR 164/GIO text and their status are unresolved. Do not merge a training rule into a general SR 114 conclusion.
Simple Terms (Hinglish)
  • Transfer ya Tour — sirf din gin kar faisla nahi: Written order dekho: posting ka purpose/nature, approved period, extension/revision, station aur residence facts. Phir current SR 114 aur applicable instructions check karo. Is guide ne current full SR 114 text verify nahi kiya.
  • Example: 5 months (150 days) ka temporary-duty order apne aap “Tour, no CTG” prove nahi karta; 7 months bhi duration alone se poora transfer package prove nahi karta. Exact order aur operative rule ki zaroorat hai.
  • Training: SR 164 ke training rules alag hain; secondary references current primary text ka badal nahi. Is guide mein training-specific schedule/option bhi held hai.
  • Exam caution: Sirf duration di ho aur controlling order/conditions na diye hon, to answer ko settled key na samjho. Q21 isi wajah se unscored hai.
RED-FLAG TRAP

Flag: “Under 180 = Tour; over 180 = Transfer.” Fix: This duration-only shorthand was not verified in the current Central primary text retrieved. Do not classify a case or grant/deny CTG, baggage or family travel from duration alone; check the actual order and applicable SR 114 conditions.

Same City / Urban Agglomeration (UA) — Transfer Within the Same Station

What happens when an officer is moved from one office building to another within the same city (e.g., from North Block to South Block in Delhi, or from one campus to another in Bengaluru)?

When an officer is moved between offices in the same city or Urban Agglomeration (UA), keep the residence fact distinct from the station boundary:

  • No change of residence: If the move is within the same UA and does not involve an actual change of residence, the guide states no TA is payable (CTG nil; no fare reimbursement).
  • With change of residence within the same UA: The move is treated as a transfer within the same station. The cited rule gives one-third of normal CTG (1/3 × 80% = 26.67% of last basic pay), plus actual transportation charges for personal effects within the city.
  • Station boundary: The guide describes “station” for TA purposes as the entire Census-defined UA, not merely the municipal boundary; its example is Delhi UA including Noida, Gurugram, Faridabad and Ghaziabad.

Exam illustration: A North Block-to-South Block move (3 km) in Delhi with a residence change is shown as one-third CTG, not nil or full CTG, because both offices are within the same UA. Verify the case facts and applicable order before applying.

Very High-YieldTransfer TA — Spousal Scaling, Multiple Road Mileages, and Tax Reimbursements (SR 116)

Exact-Source Fact Card

Under Swamy's Handbook-2024 and GIOs below SR 116, several critical administrative and financial nuances govern Travelling Allowance on Transfer:

  • 1. Multiple Road Mileages for Road Travel (GIO (5), Para IV, SR 116):
    If the transfer journey is performed between places not connected by rail otherwise than by public bus (e.g. by private car or hired vehicle), road mileage is payable at scaled rates based on the number of family members travelling:
    • One Road Mileage: For self, or self and one additional family member.
    • Two Road Mileages: If exactly two family members accompany the Government servant.
    • Three Road Mileages: If more than two family members accompany the Government servant.
  • 2. Reimbursement of Service Tax, GST, and Cess (GIO (10), SR 116):
    All elements of Service Tax, GST, and Education Cess charged by transport operators in the bill for the transportation of personal effects are fully reimbursable up to the employee's maximum entitlement ceiling.
  • 3. Conveyance Transport "Public Interest" Exemption (Item V, GIO (1), SR 116):
    For claiming the cost of transporting a personal vehicle (car/scooter) on transfer, the possession of the conveyance at the old or new station need not be in the public interest (reimbursable as long as it is owned strictly by the employee).
  • 4. Department of Posts Choice Posting Concession (SR 114):
    Under postal-specific GIOs, a posting to a place of choice on the completion of a full tenure is not treated as a transfer on own request. Consequently, such officials are fully entitled to Transfer TA and CTG under SR 114.
Simple Terms (Hinglish)
  • Transfer TA ke Swamy's Handbook ke 4 Special Rules (SR 116):
  • Rule 1 — Family ke count se mileage multiply hota hai: Agar aapka naya station train se connected nahi hai (jaise koi remote NE area) aur aapko private car/taxi se family ko le kar jaana hai, to road mileage family members ke count ke hisaab se badhti hai: Akele ya +1 member = 1× rate (₹30/km), 2 members = 2× rate (₹60/km), 3+ members = 3× rate (₹90/km). Example: 100 km journey, 3 family members → 100 × ₹90 = ₹9,000 mileage. Kyunki badi family ke liye badi gaadi chahiye (Innova jaisi), to zyada paisa lagta hai — sarkar ye consider karti hai.
  • Rule 2 — GST/Taxes fully reimbursable: Saman shift karne wale transporter ka bill mein jo GST, Service Tax, ya Education Cess laga hai — wo poora reimburse hoga (aapki entitlement limit ke andar). Example: Agar packing + transport bill ₹50,000 hai aur uspe ₹9,000 GST hai, to total ₹59,000 reimburse hoga (agar ye aapki weight/distance entitlement ke andar hai). Purane rules mein GST reimbursable nahi tha — ye 7th CPC ka improvement hai.
  • Rule 3 — Gaadi "public interest" mein hona zaroori nahi: Apni car ya scooter ko naye station pe train se bhejne ka paisa claim karne ke liye aapko ye prove nahi karna ki "ye gaadi sarkari duty ke liye zaroori hai." Bas gaadi aapke naam pe registered honi chahiye — bas. Chahe aap gaadi sirf weekend pe ghumne ke liye use karte ho — phir bhi transport charges milenge. Note: Gaadi spouse ke naam pe nahi honi chahiye — strictly employee ke naam pe.
  • Rule 4 — Post Office Choice Posting: Department of Posts (India Post) mein ek special rule hai: agar kisi postal employee ka tenure complete hone ke baad choice posting milti hai (jaise "aapne 5 saal Guwahati mein poore kiye, ab aapki pasand ka station — Lucknow — mil gaya"), to ise "own request transfer" nahi maana jaata. Iska matlab: full Transfer TA + full CTG milega. Normally own request transfer pe CTG kam milta hai — lekin ye exception hai.

High-YieldTransportation of personal effects (packing grant) & conveyance

Exact-Source Fact Card

Transportation of personal effects by road is reimbursed at currently applicable (25%-enhanced) rates of ₹62.50/km (Level 6 and above), ₹31.25/km (Level 5), and ₹18.75/km (Level 4 and below). By train/steamer, entitlement is by weight: Level 12 and above / Level 6–11 → 6,000 kg (by goods train/4-wheeler wagon/1 container — double container for Level 12+, single container for Level 6–11); Level 5 → 3,000 kg; Level 4 and below → 1,500 kg. Separately, transportation of a conveyance (motor car, motor cycle, scooter, moped, bicycle) is reimbursed: Level 6 and above may transport 1 motor car/similar or 1 motor cycle/scooter; Level 5 and below may transport 1 motorcycle/scooter/moped/bicycle (SR 116). Possession of the conveyance at the old or new station need not be shown to be in the public interest.

Pay LevelBy Train/SteamerBy Road (Current Rate)
12 and above6,000 kg (double container)₹62.50/km
6 to 116,000 kg (single container)₹62.50/km
53,000 kg₹31.25/km
4 and below1,500 kg₹18.75/km
Analogy

Think of the personal-effects entitlement like an airline baggage allowance that scales with "class of ticket" (Pay Level) — senior levels get a bigger "container," junior levels get a smaller weight cap.

Moderate-YieldSpecial-region transfers & TA entitlement basis on transfer

Exact-Source Fact Card

In case of transfer from one post to another, TA entitlement is determined on the basis of the pay of the lower of the two posts/grades (i.e., old vs new). Special provisions for TA on transfer to/from the North-Eastern Region, Union Territories of Andaman & Nicobar, Lakshadweep Islands and Ladakh were clarified/enhanced vide O.M. No.19030/1/2017-E.IV dated 16.06.2022.

Memory Hook

"On transfer, you're paid like the junior of your two posts — never the senior."

Master Fact-Sheet — Chapter 5

ItemValueReference
CTG general80% of last basic pay (≥20 km)O.M. 13.07.2017

Review Protocol

Active recall: (1) What CTG % applies for an eligible transfer of 15 km with and without a residence change? Of 500 km? Of Delhi-to-Port Blair? (2) What weight is a Level-9 officer entitled to transport by goods train? (3) On transfer, is TA based on the old post's pay or the new post's pay? (4) Which transfer-order facts and current rule determine Transfer versus Tour? Do not infer from duration alone. (5) What CTG applies for a same-city transfer with change of residence?

Rapid-Revision Recap — Chapter 5

  • CTG: 80% for eligible transfers ≥20 km; below 20 km/same station, 1/3 of normal CTG only if residence actually changes, otherwise nil; 100% for the specified A&N/Lakshadweep transfers.
  • Spouse-transfer rule: full CTG to first-mover, 50% to second (within 6 months, gap >60 days).
  • Personal effects by road: ₹62.50/₹31.25/₹18.75 per km (Level 6+/5/≤4, Current).
  • By train: 6,000 kg (Level 6+), 3,000 kg (Level 5), 1,500 kg (Level ≤4).
  • TA on transfer = pay of the lower of old/new post.
  • Special enhanced provisions for NE Region/A&N/Lakshadweep/Ladakh (O.M. 16.06.2022).
  • Transfer vs Tour: Check the actual order and current SR 114 conditions; 180 days alone is not a verified universal classification rule.
  • Same UA transfer: No residence change → CTG Nil; With residence change → 1/3rd CTG (26.67% of Basic Pay).
Chapter 6

TA on Retirement, Death-in-Service & Re-employment

Chapter study mapPARTIAL
FocusDistinguish retirement, death-in-service and re-employment rules and their different triggers.
Source reviewThe retirement claim window and retirement CTG were checked; other death-in-service and re-employment provisions need exact FR/SR/OM text.
Study moveRecord claimant category and trigger date first; do not transfer one deadline to another.

Very High-YieldTime limits — general vs retirement vs death-in-service

Exact-Source Fact Card

Ordinary tour/transfer/training TA claims must be submitted within 60 days succeeding the applicable trigger under GFR Rule 290 and DoE O.M. 13.03.2018, whether or not an advance was drawn; para 2 sets separate triggers for relevant family journeys and delivered personal effects/conveyances. TA on retirement has a distinct 180-day window under DoE O.M. 15.06.2021. These are claim-submission limits, not advance-adjustment deadlines. Where a Government servant dies in service, SR 148 separately addresses a family TA advance; do not import the ordinary tour-advance rules.

Claim typeTime limitReference
Ordinary TA claim submission (tour/transfer/training)60 days succeeding the applicable trigger, whether or not an advance was drawnGFR Rule 290; DoE O.M. 13.03.2018 (para 2 has separate triggers)
TA on Retirement180 daysO.M. 19030/1/2017-E.IV, 15.06.2021
Advance to family on death-in-serviceUp to 75% of likely TA claimSecondary compilation, cross-checked
RED-FLAG TRAP

Flag: "All TA claims must be filed within 60 days, no exceptions." Fix: Retirement TA claims get a specific relaxed window of 180 days — three times longer than the general rule, recognising the disruption of retirement. Fix-phrase: "60 is the rule, 180 is the retirement relief."

Very High-YieldRetirement TA Exclusions & Station Boundaries (SR 147, SR 148)

Exact-Source Fact Card

Under Swamy's Handbook-2024 and GIOs below SR 147 and SR 148, Travelling Allowance on Retirement is governed by strict boundary and eligibility rules:

  • 1. Strict Forfeiture on Resignation, Dismissal, or Removal:
    The retirement TA concession is admissible only to employees who retire on retiring pension, superannuation, invalid, or compensation pension.
    The Restriction: It is strictly NOT admissible to employees who quit service by resignation, or who are dismissed or removed from service as a disciplinary penalty.
  • 2. Last Station of Duty Boundary Definition:
    For the purpose of determining "same-station" or "change-of-residence" boundaries on transfer or retirement, the "last station of duty" strictly means and includes all areas falling within the Urban Agglomeration (UA) of that station. Moving within the UA without a change of residence does not attract CTG.
Simple Terms (Hinglish)
  • Retirement TA kis-kis ko milega aur kis-kis ko nahi? Ye bohot clearly defined hai — aur exams mein bohot test hota hai:
  • KO MILEGA (Eligible): (1) Superannuation pe retire hone wale (normal retirement at 60 years), (2) Retiring pension pe retire hone wale (voluntary retirement after qualifying service), (3) Invalid pension pe retire hone wale (medical grounds pe), (4) Compensation pension pe retire hone wale (post abolition etc.). Basically agar aapko pension mil rahi hai, to retirement TA bhi milega.
  • NAHI MILEGA (Not Eligible — Strictly): (1) Resignation (istifa) dene wale — chahe 20 saal service ho, agar resignation diya to retirement TA nahi milega. Reason: Resignation voluntary hai — aap khud chhod rahe ho, sarkar nahi nikaal rahi. (2) Dismissal ya Removal — disciplinary action se naukri se nikalne wale. (3) Temporary employees with <3 years service — agar temporary ho aur 3 saal se kam service hai, to nahi milega.
  • Station Boundary — Urban Agglomeration (UA): "Last station of duty" ka matlab sirf office ka building nahi — poori Urban Agglomeration shamil hai. Example: Agar aapka office Delhi ke North Block mein hai, to "last station" mein poora Delhi UA aata hai — Noida, Gurugram, Faridabad, Ghaziabad sab shamil hain. Agar aap retirement ke baad North Block se Gurugram shift hote ho (same UA mein), to CTG tab tak nahi milega jab tak actual ghar na badla ho.
  • Real-life scenario: Ek officer 30 saal service ke baad resign de deta hai (kisi private company mein jaane ke liye). Kya usko retirement TA milega? → Nahi! Resignation = no retirement TA, even with 30 years of service. Agar wahi officer superannuation pe retire hota (60 years complete karke), to full TA milta. Ye distinction bohot important hai.

High-YieldRe-employed persons & other retirement-adjacent clarifications

Exact-Source Fact Card

Concessions to persons re-employed in Central Government service regarding payment of TA were clarified vide O.M. No.19030/4/2020-E.IV dated 24.01.2022. TA/DA for Attendant/Escort accompanying a Government servant with disabilities during tour/training was separately provided for vide O.M. No.19030/3/2013-E.IV dated 17.02.2015. Government servants participating as players/athletes/managers/coaches/masseurs/doctors in sporting events of national/international importance within India are allowed to travel by Rail in First Class (or Economy Class by Air if travelling outside India), regardless of their ordinary pay-level entitlement.

Memory Hook

"R-A-S: Re-employed (2022), Attendant/Escort (2015), Sportspersons (First Class rail) — three special-category clarifications."

Revision of TA Bills — The 3-Year Limit (GFR Procedural Rule)

After a TA bill has been passed and payment made, it may still be revised under certain circumstances:

• Grounds for Revision: A passed TA bill can be revised for (a) arithmetic errors discovered post-payment, (b) subsequent increase in DA that retrospectively affects the rates applicable at the time of the journey, or (c) supplementary claims that were inadvertently omitted from the original bill.

• Time Limit: Under GFR provisions, a revised/supplementary TA claim can be processed within three (3) years from the date of the original claim. Beyond 3 years, no revision or supplementary claim is entertained.

• Supplementary Claim: An official who has already drawn TA on a bill may draw a supplementary claim even after the original bill has been passed — for example, if a DA rate revision occurred after the original bill was settled, or if a component was missed in the original claim.

Exam Trap: "A TA bill passed in March 2023 can be revised in June 2026 for a DA enhancement." → Yes, because 3 years and 3 months have passed, this would be time-barred. But if the revision is sought in February 2026 (within 3 years), it is admissible.

TA for Pensioners / Retired Employees Called Back for Duty

When a retired Government employee (pensioner) is summoned by a Central Government office — not for re-employment, but for specific purposes like:

• Testifying in a departmental inquiry or vigilance case,
• Appearing before a Medical Board for disability reassessment,
• Providing evidence or deposition in a court case related to their official service,

they are entitled to TA and DA as per their original pay level at the time of retirement for the duration of the visit. The travel fare (by the class they were entitled to at retirement) and Daily Allowance for the journey and halt days are reimbursable.

Important: This entitlement is distinct from re-employment (covered under O.M. 24.01.2022). A pensioner called for a one-off inquiry is not "re-employed" — they are summoned as a witness/deponent and are reimbursed accordingly.

Note: Pensioners do NOT get LTC (Leave Travel Concession) — LTC is a benefit for serving employees only.

Exam Trap: "A retired Level 11 officer is called to depose in a CBI inquiry. What TA do they get?" → Answer: TA/DA as per Level 11 (their retirement pay level), not as per any re-employment scale.

Master Fact-Sheet — Chapter 6

Review Protocol

Active recall: (1) How long after retirement can you still submit your TA claim? (2) What % TA advance can a deceased employee's family draw? (3) What's special about sportspersons' rail entitlement?

Rapid-Revision Recap — Chapter 6

  • Ordinary tour/transfer/training TA claim submission: 60 days, whether or not an advance was drawn. Retirement TA: separate 180-day window (O.M. 15.06.2021). Advance adjustment is not this claim clock.
  • Death-in-service: family can draw TA advance up to 75% of likely claim.
  • Re-employed persons' TA concession clarified 24.01.2022.
  • Attendant/Escort for disabled employee's TA: O.M. 17.02.2015.
  • Sportspersons get First Class rail regardless of pay level.
  • TA bill revision / supplementary claims: Admissible within 3 years of the original claim (GFR provisions).
  • TA for Pensioners: Retired employees summoned for inquiries/depositions get TA/DA at their retirement pay level.
