Source-status warningSR 164 training claims — do not apply an unverified universal rule

Targeted primary/secondary-source review · 22.11.2026

Verification gate: STOP. The current official SR 164/GIO compilation and the original historical orders beneath SR 164 were not retrieved in verifiable primary form. Secondary compilations and reproduced copies identify issues to check, but they do not support a categorical current claim or exam answer. Official DoE O.M. 01.02.2018 is a separate instruction for free boarding/lodging; it does not, by itself, resolve the distinct fixed-rate residential-training allowance.

  • Ordinary training DA schedules: Secondary transcriptions describe full DA for the first 180 days where boarding/lodging are not provided, and first 30 days full plus next 150 days half where institute facilities exist. The current primary text, conditions, and status were not verified.
  • Compulsory fixed-rate residential programmes: A separate historical special allowance is transcribed as actual boarding/lodging expenditure plus one-quarter of full DA for outstation participants, and actual boarding/lodging expenditure only for local participants. A secondary ISTM record cites DoPT O.M. dated 12.01.1988 (reported as No. 14011/5/87-T.N.P.); the signed primary order and its current status were not located. Its interaction with DoE O.M. 01.02.2018 remains open.
  • Courses exceeding 180 days / option: Some secondary compilations report an option between a transfer-TA route and a tour-TA/DA route for qualifying courses. Do not convert that into an automatic transfer classification or assume full family, baggage, conveyance, or CTG entitlements without the governing primary text and sanction. The purported DoP memo dated 21.05.2024 is a secondary-hosted copy; it cites “Decision No. 4 below Rule 164” for a course not exceeding 180 days, and does not resolve the >180-day option. The GIO/Decision labels in secondary sources are not consistent and must not be treated as interchangeable.
  • Theory and practical training at different stations: Secondary transcriptions of P&T orders from 1982/1984 describe a fresh-course treatment at the practical-training station in a limited in-service/refresher setting. The original letters, exact conditions, current status, and applicability to other departments/candidates were not verified. Do not generalize this to a fresh 180-day limit at every station change.
  • Tour during training: A secondary transcription associates a 17.10.1979 order with a tour to another station not terminating the halt at the training station for DA determination. This does not, without the original text, establish a general full-DA entitlement at the tour station or a universal rule for counting days away.
  • Training at headquarters / Urban Agglomeration: Secondary training material describes no TA/DA for training at the headquarters station. The current primary wording was not verified. The separate claim that this extends to the entire Urban Agglomeration is not established; no UA-wide ban is stated here.

Exam and claim use: Do not score a general second-station reset or apply a universal 30/150 schedule. Verify the current primary SR/GIO, relevant department/cadre order, training sanction, and facility/cost facts; refer disputed claims to the competent authority. TA-COV-17 remains pending [D].

Sources: DoE TA O.M., 13.07.2017 · DoE O.M., 01.02.2018 · purported DoP 21.05.2024 memo, secondary reproduction only.

Exam-safe takeaway

Training status is not established by duration or station change alone. Keep ordinary schedules, free boarding/lodging, the reported fixed-rate special allowance, and the limited P&T theory/practical orders separate. No general station-reset answer is verified.

Very High-YieldThe Absolute Spousal Rules under CCS TA & LTC (When Both are Central Government Servants)

Exact-Source Fact Card

Under the Central Civil Services (CCS) TA and LTC rules (governed by Supplementary Rules (SR) and DoPT guidelines), the entitlements when both husband and wife are Central Government servants are subject to highly specific restrictions and concessions to prevent double billing while protecting individual rights:

  • 1. Transfer TA — Composite Transfer Grant (CTG) Split (The 60-day / 6-Month Rules):
    If both spouses are transferred from the same old station to the same new station within six (6) months:
    • Transfers ordered more than 60 days apart: The spouse transferred earlier gets full CTG (80% of Basic Pay), and the spouse transferred later gets half (50% of the normal grant, which equals 40% of Basic Pay).
    • Transfers ordered within 60 days of each other: NO Composite Transfer Grant is admissible to the spouse transferred later! Only the first spouse receives the grant.
    • Note: If the transfers take place after a period of 6 months or more, both are treated as independent, and both get full CTG.
  • 2. Transfer TA — Baggage & Family Fares Double-Dipping Ban:
    • The cost of travel for dependent family members and the transportation of personal effects can be claimed only once by either husband or wife.
    • Combined Weight Ceiling: If they are transferred at different times, they can transport their share, but the total weight of personal effects transported by both combined cannot exceed the maximum entitlement of the senior-most spouse (e.g., restricted to a maximum of 6,000 kg).
    • The Double Car Exception: If both spouses own a personal car separately (registered in their respective individual names), they are entitled to claim transportation charges for both cars if they travel separately!
  • 3. Retirement TA — Settle at Same vs. Different Stations (SR 147):
    • Settle at the Same Place: They can claim fares for themselves separately. However, for family members, only one can claim, and the combined personal effects weight must be restricted to the senior spouse's limit (e.g. 6,000 kg).
    • Settle at Different Places: If they choose to settle at different stations, they can claim TA for their respective journeys and personal effects separately, up to their respective individual entitlements.
  • 4. Leave Travel Concession (LTC) Spousal Rules:
    • Separate Home Towns: They can, at their option, declare separate Home Towns and claim LTC separately under the normal provisions of CCS (LTC) Rules.
    • The Dependent Double-Dipping Ban: A family member/child can be claimed as dependent only by one spouse in a particular block year. A child cannot avail of LTC twice in a block (once as the husband's dependent and once as the wife's).
    • Claiming Spouse as Dependent: A spouse can be claimed as a dependent family member by the other spouse, provided that the spouse being claimed does not claim LTC for themselves separately during that same block.
    • Leave Encashment (10 days): Both spouses are entitled to 10 days of earned leave encashment at the time of availing LTC separately, subject to a career maximum of 60 days each.
Simple Terms (Hinglish)
  • Husband-Wife dono Govt Employees — TA & LTC ke rules: Ye complex zone hai lekin logically samjho:
  • Transfer CTG Split: Agar dono ka same station se same station transfer ho within 6 months:
    • Transfers 60 din ke andar: Pehle wale ko full CTG, dusre ko ZERO CTG!
    • Transfers 60 din se 6 mahine ke beech: Pehle wale ko Full (80%), dusre ko Half (40%).
    • 6 mahine ke baad: Dono ko full CTG independently.
    Logic: Ek hi ghar shift ho raha hai — dono ko full CTG dena double-dipping hoga.
  • Baggage Weight Ceiling: Saman ka weight dono ka mila ke senior spouse ki maximum limit se zyada nahi ho sakta (e.g., 6,000 kg max). Lekin double car exception: Agar dono ke paas apni alag-alag registered cars hain, to dono transport karwa sakte hain.
  • Retirement — Same vs Different Cities: Same city settle → combined weight limit. Different cities settle → dono independently full TA claim kar sakte hain.
  • LTC — Separate Home Towns: Dono alag-alag Home Town declare kar sakte hain. Bachche kisi ek parent ke sath claim honge — double claim nahi. Ek spouse dusre ko dependent dikha ke LTC le sakta hai — agar dusra spouse khud alag se LTC na le. 10 days Leave Encashment dono ko alag-alag milega.
Memory Hook

"Separate but No Double-Dipping. Husband and wife can act independently (separate cars, separate retirement cities, separate LTC home towns), but they cannot claim the same child, same family member, or same baggage weight twice!"

Very High-YieldTA to Family on Death-in-Service — Absolute Eligibility, Surety and Recovery Rules (SR 148)

Exact-Source Fact Card

In the tragic event of a Government servant dying while in service, the concessions and advance of Travelling Allowance for the family to settle at their normal place of residence or home town are governed under SR 148 with very strict financial rules:

  • 1. Absolute Eligibility Exclusions (Rule 6, SR 148):
    The death-in-service TA concession is strictly NOT admissible to the families of:
    • (a) Employees who die while on leave preparatory to retirement.
    • (b) Retired employees who are re-employed in government service.
    • (c) Temporary employees who have not rendered at least three (3) years of regular continuous service.
  • 2. Surety Requirement for TA Advance (GIO (3), SR 148):
    The family can draw a TA advance up to three-fourths (75%) of the probable amount of travel expenses.
    The Security Rule: To draw this advance, the family must provide a surety of a permanent Central Government employee of comparable or higher status! Only one family member can receive the advance on behalf of all, and an undertaking for recovery must be signed.
  • 3. Strictly Enforced Journey & Recovery Timelines:
    The journey of the family to the settlement place must be completed within one year (365 days) from the date of the employee's death.
    The Adjustment Deadline (Bada Trap):
    • If the family travels in one batch: The final adjustment claim must be submitted within one month of the completion of the journey.
    • If the family travels in more than one batch: The final adjustment claim must be submitted within one month of the completion of the journey by the last batch.
    • If the account is not rendered within one month of the journey, the entire advance must be refunded immediately.
Simple Terms (Hinglish)
  • Death-in-Service — Parivar ko TA (SR 148): Tragically agar kisi employee ki service mein death ho jaaye, to family ko ghar settle karne ka TA milta hai — lekin strict rules hain:
  • Exclusions — Kise nahi milega: (a) Jo leave preparatory to retirement pe the unki death pe nahi. (b) Re-employed retired employees ki death pe nahi. (c) Temporary employees with <3 years service ki death pe nahi. Ye 3 exclusions yaad rakho — exams mein aate hain.
  • Advance — 75% with Surety: Family 75% of likely TA claim advance le sakta hai — lekin ek permanent govt employee ki surety (guarantee) chahiye jo comparable ya higher status ka ho. Sirf ek family member advance receive kar sakta hai — sabke behalf pe.
  • Deadlines: Journey 1 saal (365 din) ke andar complete karni hai death date se. Bill 1 mahine (30 din) ke andar submit karna hai journey complete hone ke baad. Agar family multiple batches mein travel karti hai, to last batch ki journey ke 1 mahine ke andar bill dena hai.

Very High-YieldDoE O.M. No. 12(1)/2016-E.II(A) — Immediate Relief Advance on Demise of Government Servant

Exact-Source Fact Card

Under Ministry of Finance, Department of Expenditure O.M. No. 12(1)/2016-E.II(A) dated 20th June, 2018 (read with Controller General of Accounts O.M. No. TA-2-03002(01)/6/2021 TA-II/383 dated 30th April 2021), the immediate financial assistance provided to the family of a Government servant who dies while in service is strictly regulated under the Compendium of Rules on Advances:

  • 1. The Legal Authority:
    The advance is granted under Rule 79 and Rule 80 of the Compendium of Rules on Advances to Government Servants, 2005 to meet immediate funeral, medical, and emergency expenditures of the family upon the sudden demise of the employee.
  • 2. The Amount of Immediate Relief:
    The immediate relief advance is a fixed sum of ₹25,000 (enhanced significantly under the 7th CPC from the legacy limit of ₹1,000 or 3 months' pay, whichever was less).
  • 3. Expeditious Cash Disbursement (O.M. dated 30.04.2021):
    To ensure that the family gets financial assistance without bureaucratic or bank-clearance delays, Drawing and Disbursing Officers (DDOs) are permitted to draw and disburse this ₹25,000 in CASH immediately, utilizing the office Imprest, other internal office resources, or by directly drawing cash from the bank.
  • 4. Strict Recovery and Adjustment:
    This immediate relief of ₹25,000 is an advance and must be strictly adjusted and recovered by the DDO against the first available settlement arrears of pay and allowances, leave salary, Death Gratuity, GPF/CPF balances, or any other final terminal payments due in respect of the deceased Government servant.
Simple Terms (Hinglish)
  • ₹25,000 Immediate Relief — Death pe turant cash! (O.M. 20.06.2018 + CGA O.M. 30.04.2021):
  • Kya hai? Agar kisi govt employee ki service mein death ho jaati hai, to family ko turant ₹25,000 cash milta hai — funeral aur emergency kharchon ke liye. Ye pehle ₹1,000 ya 3 months' pay tha — 7th CPC ne significantly enhance kiya.
  • Cash mein kaise milta hai? 30 April 2021 ke CGA order se DDO ko permission mili hai ki wo ye ₹25,000 CASH mein turant disburse kar sake — office ke Imprest cash se ya bank se nikal ke. Bank transfer ka wait nahi karna — family ko turant paisa milna chahiye.
  • Adjustment (Recovery): Ye ek advance hai — to baad mein family ko milne wale terminal payments se adjust hoga: Death Gratuity, GPF balance, Leave Encashment, salary arrears — jo bhi milega usme se ₹25,000 minus honge.
  • Memory Hook: "Funeral is Fast, Cash is ₹25K" — tragedy mein DDO bank transfer ka wait nahi karta, haath mein cash deta hai.
Memory Hook

"Funeral is Fast, Cash is ₹25K. When tragedy strikes, the DDO bypasses bank transfers and hands ₹25,000 in cash to the family, adjusting it later against the gratuity or GPF settlement."

Very High-YieldEffect of free transport / boarding / lodging on Daily Allowance (7th CPC Reduction Rules)

Exact-Source Fact Card

Under the 7th CPC (clarified via Department of Expenditure O.M. No. 19030/1/2017-E.IV dated 01.02.2018), the old concept of flat-rate Daily Allowance is replaced by three independent reimbursement components: (1) Hotel Accommodation, (2) Food Bills, and (3) Local Travel/Conveyance. Consequently, when an employee avails free facilities, the DA is scaled down component-wise:

  • 1. Free Lodging Provided: If the employee is provided free stay (e.g. Government Guest House, Circuit House, or Host accommodation), the Hotel Accommodation component is reduced to NIL (no claim allowed).
  • 2. Free Boarding Provided: If the employee is provided free meals (e.g. by the Government, Host Organization, or during residential training), the Food Bill component is reduced to NIL (no claim allowed).
  • 3. Free Transport Provided: If the employee is provided free local transport (official vehicle/pool car), the Local Travel/Conveyance component is reduced to NIL.
  • 4. When BOTH Free Boarding & Lodging are Provided:
    • Both Hotel and Food components are reduced to NIL.
    • The employee can only claim actual/capped local travel charges (as per Para 2E(i) & (iii) of the 13.07.2017 OM) for local journeys actually performed on official duty (e.g., travel from airport/railway station to guest house) which were not covered by free transport.
    • CRITICAL UPDATE: The old 6th CPC system of granting a flat 25% of the Daily Allowance when boarding and lodging were free has been completely discontinued. There is no flat percentage anymore; if stay and food are free, those components are zeroed out.
Simple Terms (Hinglish)
  • Free facilities pe DA kaise adjust hota hai? (Component-wise Zeroing):
  • 7th CPC ka naya system: Pehle flat DA tha — ab 3 independent components hain (Hotel, Food, Travel). Har component independently zero hota hai agar wo facility free hai:
  • • Free stay (Lodging): Hotel claim = ZERO (NIL).
    • Free food (Boarding): Food claim = ZERO (NIL).
    • Free transport: Local travel claim = ZERO (NIL).
    • Dono stay + food free: Hotel + Food = dono ZERO. Sirf local travel claim kar sakte ho (agar actually use kiya — jaise airport se guest house tak ka auto).
  • Purana 25% rule KHTAM: 6th CPC mein jab dono free the, to 25% flat DA milta tha out-of-pocket ke liye. 01.02.2018 ke O.M. ne ye completely band kar diya. Ab zero DA milega stay/food ke liye — sirf actual local travel.
  • Example: Level 9 officer residential training pe (free hostel + mess). Normal DA = ₹2,812.50 + ₹1,125 + ₹422.50 = ₹4,360/day. Ab kya milega? Hotel NIL + Food NIL + Travel ₹422.50 (if used) = sirf ₹422.50/day.
RED-FLAG TRAP

Flag: "If both boarding and lodging are provided free, the employee gets 25% of the Daily Allowance."
Fix: This is an outdated 6th CPC rule. Under the latest 7th CPC orders (DoE O.M. dated 01.02.2018), the 25% flat DA is completely discontinued. If boarding and lodging are free, the Hotel and Food DA components are reduced to NIL, and only actual local travel expenses under Para 2E are admissible. Fix-phrase: "Free stay + food = Zero stay/food DA; only local travel is payable."

High-YieldDA admissibility for the day of departure/return on overnight journeys

Exact-Source Fact Card

Under Para 2E(v) of the 13.07.2017 O.M., the lump-sum Daily Allowance (Food component) is regulated strictly based on the length of absence from headquarters, calculated on a calendar-day basis (midnight to midnight):

Length of Absence (on a calendar day)Food DA Percentage Admissible
Less than 6 hours (< 6 hrs)30% of the lump-sum ceiling
Between 6 to 12 hours (6–12 hrs)70% of the lump-sum ceiling
More than 12 hours (> 12 hrs)100% of the lump-sum ceiling

For overnight train or air journeys, the day of departure and the day of return are calculated strictly based on these time-slab percentages according to the exact departure/arrival times:

  • Day of Departure (Overnight Journey): Since the journey starts on a calendar day, the absence starts from the actual departure time of the train/flight until midnight (24:00 hours) of that day.
  • Day of Return (Overnight Journey): The absence is counted from midnight (00:00 hours) of the return day until the actual arrival time of the train/flight at headquarters.
Worked Example

Scenario: A Level 11 Officer (Food DA ceiling: ₹1,125/day) travels on official tour. They board an overnight train departing at 20:00 (8:00 PM) on Monday, spend all of Tuesday at the tour station, and arrive back at headquarters at 08:30 AM on Wednesday.
Step 1: Calculate Monday (Day of Departure)
Absence on Monday is from 20:00 to 24:00 (midnight) = 4 hours.
Since 4 hours is < 6 hours, Monday's DA is restricted to 30% of ₹1,125.
Monday DA = ₹1,125 × 30% = ₹337.50.
Step 2: Calculate Tuesday (Full Day on Tour)
The officer is away for the entire 24 hours on Tuesday (> 12 hours) = 100% of ₹1,125.
Tuesday DA = ₹1,125.00.
Step 3: Calculate Wednesday (Day of Return)
Absence on Wednesday is from midnight (00:00) to 08:30 AM = 8.5 hours.
Since 8.5 hours is between 6 and 12 hours, Wednesday's DA is 70% of ₹1,125.
Wednesday DA = ₹1,125 × 70% = ₹787.50.
Total Food DA Claimed: ₹337.50 (Mon) + ₹1,125 (Tue) + ₹787.50 (Wed) = ₹2,250.00.

High-YieldTA for departmental exams / LDCE & recruitment interviews

Exact-Source Fact Card

Travel entitlements for appearing in examinations and recruitment interviews are distinctly categorized based on employment status:

  • 1. Serving Government Employees appearing in Departmental Exams / LDCE (SR 110, SR 111):
    • Obligatory Exams (SR 110): If appearing in an obligatory departmental exam (mandatory for confirmation/promotion), the journey is treated as on duty. Full TA as on tour (travel fare per their Pay Level + DA for the journey days) is admissible.
    • Selection/Promotion Exams / LDCE (SR 111): If appearing in a competitive departmental exam (e.g. SO LDCE) with proper authorization, TA is also admissible as on tour.
    • CRITICAL LIMITATION (SR 113): No Daily Allowance (DA) is admissible for halts at the examination center. Only travel fare and DA for the journey days (days of transit) are paid.
    • Attempt Limit: TA is restricted to a maximum of two attempts for any particular examination. No TA is paid for third or subsequent attempts.
  • 2. Candidates called for Recruitment Interviews (Outsiders/Unemployed):
    • Serving Employees: Sponsoring/serving employees summoned for interview by UPSC/SSC or other departments are treated as on duty and get TA as on tour (travel fare + journey and halt DA) paid by their parent department.
    • Unemployed / Outsider Candidates (especially SC/ST): Candidates called for written test/interview by UPSC, SSC, or Central Ministries are entitled to second-class / sleeper-class train fare (or bus fare) by the shortest route, provided the distance exceeds a specified limit (usually 80 km) and they are not already employed in government service. For Group-A recruitments, UPSC may allow AC 3-Tier rail fare to all called candidates.
Simple Terms (Hinglish)
  • Departmental Exams (LDCE etc.) ke liye TA — kya milega, kya nahi?
  • Serving Employees (Obligatory exams — SR 110): Agar exam mandatory hai (confirmation/promotion ke liye zaroori hai), to journey "on duty" maani jaati hai. Full Tour TA milega — travel fare (Pay Level ke hisaab se train/air ticket) + journey days ka DA.
  • Selection/Promotion Exams / LDCE (SR 111): Competitive departmental exams (jaise SO LDCE) ke liye bhi TA milta hai — proper authorization ke saath. Same rules apply — tour TA jaisa.
  • ⚠ CRITICAL — Halt DA nahi milta (SR 113): Exam center pe rehne ka DA (hotel + food) nahi milta! Sirf aane-jaane ka ticket fare aur raste ke journey days ka DA milega. Example: Aap Delhi se Lucknow LDCE dene gaye. Train fare milega, raste ka DA milega — lekin Lucknow mein 2 din ruk ke exam dene ka hotel/food DA nahi milega. Ye apna kharcha hai.
  • Maximum 2 Attempts: Kisi bhi ek exam ke liye sirf 2 attempts tak TA milega. Teesre attempt se apne kharche pe jaana hoga. Ye "no free rides forever" policy hai.
  • Outsider/Recruitment Candidates: Jo already govt employee nahi hain aur UPSC/SSC interview ke liye bulaye jaate hain — unhe Second Class (Mail/Express) train fare milta hai shortest route se — agar distance 80 km se zyada hai. SC/ST candidates ko 80 km minimum nahi lagta — unhe kisi bhi distance pe milega. UPSC Group-A interviews mein AC 3-Tier bhi mil sakta hai. Koi DA nahi milta — sirf fare.

SR 172 — TA for Non-Government Candidates Called for UPSC/SSC Interviews

Source status — unverified / do not score: Current SR 172/GIO text and applicable recruiting-body instructions were not retrieved in this review. The 80-km threshold, SC/ST exception, Group-A enhancement and fare-only scope below remain claims to verify; MCQ-TA-19 remains unscored.

Secondary descriptions of Supplementary Rule 172 (SR 172) concern non-Government candidates called for tests or interviews by UPSC, SSC or other Central Government recruiting bodies. The following points are retained as an unverified study lead, not a settled entitlement:

  • Claimed fare entitlement: Second Class (Mail/Express) train fare or ordinary bus fare by the shortest route.
  • Claimed distance condition: One-way distance from the candidate's ordinary residence to the venue must exceed 80 km.
  • Claimed SC/ST exception: Fare irrespective of the 80-km minimum.
  • Claimed UPSC Group-A enhancement: AC 3-Tier rail fare may be allowed for candidates called for the Personality Test, subject to UPSC instructions.
  • Claimed DA scope: Fare only, with no DA for halts at the examination/interview station.

Study caution, not an answer key: The 60-km example depends on the unverified threshold and exception. Do not score it or use it to decide a claim until the current SR 172 text and applicable instructions are checked.

Very High-YieldCTG on Transfer vs Retirement — Same-Station & <20km Rules (The 2022 Shift)

Exact-Source Fact Card

Composite Transfer Grant (CTG) on transfer and retirement exhibits a pivotal structural difference since the issuance of Department of Expenditure O.M. No. 19030/1/2017-E.IV dated 6th January 2022. This is one of the most heavily tested, high-yield zones for administrative exams:

CTG on transfer vs retirement — residence change is the key fact
CaseDistance / stationActual residence change?CTG treatmentSource / condition
TransferBelow 20 km or within the same city/stationNoNilDoE O.M. 13.07.2017, para 3(ii)(b).
TransferBelow 20 km or within the same city/stationYesOne-third of normal CTG (26.67% of Basic Pay, based on the 80% normal rate).DoE O.M. 13.07.2017, para 3(ii)(b).
TransferEligible change of station at 20 km or moreSubject to the transfer facts and other conditionsOrdinary CTG 80%; specified A&N/Lakshadweep transfers have a separate 100% rate.DoE O.M. 13.07.2017, para 3(ii)(a); verify remaining admissibility conditions.
RetirementAny eligible settlement location, including last station or within 20 kmNoNilDoE O.M. 06.01.2022 requires an actual residence change.
RetirementAny eligible settlement location, including last station or within 20 kmYesFull eligible CTG at 80%; no one-third reduction based solely on distance.DoE O.M. 06.01.2022; prescribed Annexure-I declaration is required.

Boundary: The 2022 retirement change does not replace the transfer rule. Confirm the actual residence change and all other eligibility conditions.

Simple Terms (Hinglish)
  • Transfer vs Retirement CTG — Bada Antar (O.M. 06.01.2022 ne badla): Ye ek bohot commonly tested comparison hai. Same city/same station pe CTG ka treatment dono cases mein alag hai:
  • Transfer pe same city mein (with residence change): Normal CTG ka sirf one-third (1/3) milta hai — yaani 1/3 × 80% = 26.67% of Basic Pay. Example: Basic pay ₹1,00,000 → CTG = 26.67% = ₹26,670. Agar residence change nahi hua → NIL.
  • Retirement pe same city mein (with residence change): FULL CTG (80% of Basic Pay) milta hai! 06.01.2022 ke O.M. ne purana 1/3rd restriction completely abolish kar diya. Example: Basic pay ₹1,00,000 → CTG = 80% = ₹80,000 — pehle sirf ₹26,670 milta, ab poora ₹80,000! Agar residence change nahi hua → NIL (same as transfer).
  • Kyun different hai? Transfer pe logic hai "same city mein to move karna easy hai, kam kharcha hoga." Lekin retirement pe sarkar ne kaha "ye banda 30-35 saal service karke retire ho raha hai — same city mein settle ho to bhi full CTG do — iska ghar shift karna bhi ek bada kaam hai."
  • Exam trap: "An officer retires and settles at the last station with change of residence. CTG = ?" Options: (a) Nil, (b) 1/3rd CTG, (c) Full 80% CTG, (d) 50% CTG. Answer: (c) Full 80% CTG — since 06.01.2022. Many candidates confuse it with transfer rules and select 1/3rd — that's the trap!
Memory Hook

"Transfer is 1/3rd, Retirement is Full. If you move within the same city on transfer, you get 1/3rd CTG. But if you retire and move within the same city, you get FULL 80% CTG!"

Exam Angle / PYQ

This is a major examiner trap! Questions will ask: "An officer retires and settles at the last station of duty involving a change of residence. What is their CTG entitlement?" — many candidates select 1/3rd of CTG based on old rules or confusing it with transfer rules. Since the 06.01.2022 O.M., the answer is 80% of Basic Pay (Full CTG).

High-YieldTA on Deputation, Absorption Rules & Probationer Training Durations

Exact-Source Fact Card

Transfer TA on deputation, absorption, and probationer training is governed under standard DoPT and Supplementary Rules (SR) with very precise cost-bearing and tenure rules:

  • 1. TA on Deputation (DoPT OM No. 6/8/2009-Estt.(Pay-II) dated 15.02.2012 / 08.09.2022):
    • Onward Journey (Joining): The Transfer TA (self/family fare, CTG, and baggage transport) is borne and paid entirely by the borrowing department/organization.
    • Return Journey (Reversion): Upon repatriation/reversion back to the parent department, the Transfer TA is also borne and paid entirely by the borrowing department/organization. This is a standard mandatory condition.
    • The Absorption Exception (No Return TA): If a deputationist is permanently absorbed in the borrowing department/organization during or at the end of their deputation, NO return TA is payable upon absorption, as there is no repatriation involved.
  • 2. Probationer / trainee training claims — use the programme-specific order:
    • Do not infer a universal “under 180 days = Tour / over 180 days = Transfer” rule, or full family/baggage/CTG entitlement, from the duration alone. The current SR 164/GIO text and the relevant training orders remain unverified.
    • A DoPT Training Division O.M. dated 21.01.2016 (T-25014/1/2016-TRG) is reproduced in ISTM-hosted material for Foundation Training of specified newly recruited ASO/Stenographer probationers. Its limits must not be generalized to every cadre or Postal Training Centre course.
    • For PTC/RAKNPA and other residential courses, verify the cadre-specific order, sanction, trainee status, and whether facilities are free or compulsory at fixed rates. The ordinary SR 164 schedule and the separate fixed-rate special-allowance question remain open; see the source-status note below.
Simple Terms (Hinglish)
  • Deputation pe TA kaun deta hai? (DoPT Guidelines): Simple principle: "Borrowing Department Pays — Both Ways!"
  • Joining ka TA: Jab aap parent department se kisi dusre department/PSU mein deputation pe jaate ho, to borrowing department (jahan kaam karne ja rahe ho) poora Transfer TA pay karega — fare, CTG, baggage, car transport — sab.
  • Return ka TA: Jab deputation khatam ho aur aap parent department wapas aate ho (reversion), to bhi borrowing department (jise chhod rahe ho) return TA pay karega. Matlab borrowing department ko dono taraf ka paisa dena hai — joining bhi, reversion bhi.
  • Absorption Exception: Agar aap deputation ke dauran borrowing department mein permanently absorb ho jaate ho, to return TA nahi milega — kyunki aap wapas ja hi nahi rahe! Aap ab permanently wahin ke ho gaye.
  • Probationer training: 180 days alone does not establish a universal Tour/Transfer classification. Follow the specific cadre/course order and sanction; check facilities and the current SR 164 text. Foundation Training instructions for one probationer group are not automatically rules for every PTC/RAKNPA course.

Moderate-YieldTransfer TA for probationers/trainees on first appointment

Exact-Source Fact Card

A person joining Government service for the first time (e.g. a direct-recruit probationer reporting to their first place of training or posting) is NOT entitled to any Transfer TA, fares, or baggage transport allowance. The cost of reporting for the first appointment must be borne by the candidate themselves. Exception: Serving Government employees who are selected for another post through direct recruitment and are appointed without a break in service are treated as transferred in public interest, and full Transfer TA is admissible.

Very High-YieldTA on Retirement — family travel scale, settlement capping, and death before journey

Exact-Source Fact Card

TA on Retirement is governed under SR 147 and provides the same entitlements as Transfer TA (Ch. 5), but is subject to unique capping and family rules:

  • Family Travel Scale: Eligible family members (as defined in Ch. 10) are entitled to travel fare reimbursement according to the retiree's Pay Matrix Level at the time of retirement, along with full CTG (80% of Basic Pay) and the transportation of personal effects/conveyance.
  • Settlement Location and Capping Rule: A retiring employee may choose to settle at their last headquarters, their declared home town, or any other place of their choice in India.
    • If settling at a place other than the last headquarters or home town, the reimbursement (fares + baggage/car transport) is strictly restricted/capped at what would have been admissible had they settled at their declared home town, or the actual expenditure incurred, whichever is less.
  • Time-Limit for Journey: The retirement journey and baggage transportation must be completed within one year (365 days) from the date of retirement. The final TA claim must be submitted within 180 days succeeding the date of completion of the journey.
  • Death after Retirement before Journey: If a retired Government servant dies after retirement but before actually performing the retirement journey:
    • Their eligible family members retain the full entitlement to draw TA on Retirement (fares, CTG, and transportation) to the declared home town or place of settlement.
    • The journey must be performed within the original one-year window from the retirement date.
Simple Terms (Hinglish)
  • Retirement TA — Family, Settlement, Capping aur Death rules:
  • Family Travel Scale: Retire hone pe aapko aur aapke dependent family members (wife/husband, bachche, dependent parents etc.) ko permanent ghar settle karne ke liye Transfer TA ke scale pe tickets, baggage (cargo) aur gaadi transport milta hai. Journey 365 din (1 saal) ke andar complete honi chahiye retirement date se.
  • ⚠ Capping Rule — Home Town is the Ceiling: Aap India mein kahin bhi settle ho sakte ho — last HQ, home town, ya koi naya shehar. Lekin agar aap home town ke alawa kisi doosre (door) shehar mein settle hote ho, to reimbursement home town tak ke calculated kharche pe cap ho jaayegi.
  • Example: Level 12 officer retires from Delhi. Home town = Srinagar (800 km). Settles in Chennai (2,200 km). Actual Chennai cost = ₹95,000. Notional Srinagar cost = ₹45,000. Result: Sirf ₹45,000 milega — baaki ₹50,000 personally bear karo. "Home Town is the Ceiling!"
  • Death after Retirement (before journey): Agar retired employee ki death journey se pehle ho jaaye, to family poora retirement TA claim kar sakti hai — original 1-year window ke andar. Retirement ka right family ko transfer ho jaata hai.
Memory Hook

"Home Town is the Ceiling. You can settle in Paris or Chennai, but your cash limit stops at Patna (Home Town). If a retiree leaves us too soon, the family still gets the ticket to tune."

Worked Example

Scenario: A Level 12 officer retires from Delhi. Their declared home town is Srinagar (distance: 800 km). They decide to settle in Chennai (distance: 2,200 km). They incur an actual travel and baggage transportation cost of ₹95,000 for the Chennai journey. The calculated notional cost to settle in their home town (Srinagar) is ₹45,000.
Step 1: Apply the Capping Principle
Retirement TA is capped at the lesser of the actual cost incurred (Chennai, ₹95,000) or the notional cost to the declared home town (Srinagar, ₹45,000).
Step 2: Comparison
Notional Srinagar Cost (₹45,000) < Actual Chennai Cost (₹95,000).
Result: The officer's reimbursement is strictly restricted to ₹45,000. The additional ₹50,000 must be borne personally by the retiree.

Source-status reviewDisability-related TA claims — separate verified and unresolved routes

Exact-Source Fact Card

The DoE escort O.M. and the DoPT LTC exception address different benefits. Neither source supports an unrestricted local AC-taxi benefit or an extra baggage-weight allowance for transfer/retirement.

  • 1. Attendant / Escort TA (O.M. No. 19030/3/2013-E.IV dated 17.02.2015):
    • The O.M. creates a conditional fare claim for an escort accompanying a Government servant with disabilities on tour/training, etc.; it is not a blanket right to a privately arranged escort in every case. Ordinarily, the destination office or institute is to provide an escort.
    • When a fare claim may arise: the destination office/institute (or Indian Mission for foreign travel) must officially express inability to provide the escort; the competent medical authority must certify that the employee compulsorily requires assistance for travel; the competent authority must approve the need, and the tour/training must meet the O.M.’s stated conditions.
    • Fare / DA: admissible escort travel is reimbursed at the same rate and in the same class as the employee, subject to concessions and applicable travel restrictions. No DA is admissible to the escort. For road conveyance, no separate escort mileage is payable except where travel is by public bus.
  • 2. Local AC taxi during official tour/training:
    The 2017 TA O.M. sets local-travel entitlements by pay level; the 22.12.2020 O.M. changes the receipt/voucher rule for Levels 9–11, not the vehicle class or level ceiling. The 17.02.2015 escort O.M. does not create an employee local-AC-taxi exemption. A general Divyang AC-taxi entitlement regardless of pay level was not verified in the Central primary sources checked; keep it on hold.
  • 3. Additional baggage on transfer/retirement:
    The claimed additional 500 kg for assistive devices has not been verified in a current Central primary source. Do not assert it or calculate it into a claim without the operative signed order. The 13.07.2017 DoE O.M. lists the ordinary personal-effects limits but does not establish this claimed supplement.
  • 4. Double Transport Allowance (daily commute, not tour TA):
    The official DoE O.M. No. 21/1/2018-E.II(B), 29.07.2025 substituted category B(1) of the 15.09.2022 compendium. Central Government employees with benchmark disabilities in the listed RPwD Act categories may receive Transport Allowance at double the normal rate, subject to the compendium’s conditions and valid disability certification: (i) locomotor disability (including leprosy-cured, cerebral palsy, dwarfism, muscular dystrophy and acid-attack victims; spinal deformity/injury are sub-categories); (ii) blindness and low vision; (iii) deaf and hard of hearing; (iv) speech and language disability; (v) intellectual disability, including specific learning disabilities and autism spectrum disorder; (vi) mental illness; (vii) chronic neurological conditions such as multiple sclerosis and Parkinson’s disease; (viii) specified blood disorders—haemophilia, thalassemia and sickle-cell disease; and (ix) multiple disabilities, including deaf-blindness. Railway employees have separate orders. This daily home-to-office allowance is not a local-tour AC-taxi or transfer-baggage entitlement.
    Illustrative 2026 arithmetic (with 63% DA): In higher-TPTA cities, the following totals are before any payroll rounding; apply the relevant normal rate and all eligibility/absence conditions:
    • Level 9 & above: (₹7,200 × 2) = ₹14,400 + 63% DA = ₹23,472 per month (regular base-rate calculation: ₹11,736).
    • Level 3 to 8: (₹3,600 × 2) = ₹7,200 + 63% DA = ₹11,736 per month (regular base-rate calculation: ₹5,868).
    • Level 1 to 2 (Basic ≥ ₹24,200): (₹3,600 × 2) = ₹7,200 + 63% DA = ₹11,736 per month.
    • Level 1 to 2 (Basic < ₹24,200): (₹1,350 × 2) = ₹2,700 + 63% DA = ₹4,401 per month (before payroll rounding).
Simple Terms (Hinglish)
  • Divyang (Disabled) Employees ke Special TA Provisions:
  • 1. Escort TA (O.M. 17.02.2015): Agar Divyang employee ko tour pe escort/sahayak ki zaroorat hai, to escort ka travel fare (same class ticket) reimburse hoga — employee ke same class mein. Lekin escort ko koi DA nahi milega — na hotel, na food. Sirf aane-jaane ka ticket. Ye ek bada trap hai — "escort ko bhi DA milega" → WRONG.
  • 2. Local AC taxi on official tour/training: Is source check mein pay-level se independent Divyang entitlement ka current Central primary order nahi mila. Ordinary pay-level terms follow karo unless applicable signed authority identify ho. LTC taxi exception alag aur conditional hai.
  • 3. Extra baggage: Assistive devices ke liye 500 kg extra entitlement current Central primary source se verify nahi hua. Signed operative order ke bina ise entitlement ya claim calculation mein include mat karo.
  • 4. Double Transport Allowance (daily commute): Ye tour ke local taxi ya transfer-baggage benefit nahi hai. DoE O.M. 29.07.2025 ne 15.09.2022 compendium ki disability list badli: benchmark disabilities mein locomotor, blindness/low vision, deaf/hard of hearing, speech/language, intellectual/SLD/autism, mental illness, chronic neurological conditions, specified blood disorders aur multiple disabilities shamil hain (spinal deformity/injury locomotor sub-category). Valid disability certificate aur baaki compendium conditions apply; Railways ke separate orders hain. 63% DA ke illustrative higher-city totals: Level 9+ ₹23,472/month; Level 3–8/eligible Level 1–2 ₹11,736/month; Level 1–2 below ₹24,200 ₹4,401/month—employee eligibility, actual rate/city, full-month absence rules aur payroll rounding verify karo.

Review Protocol

Active recall questions:
(1) What is the ordinary TA claim-submission window, and how is it distinct from advance adjustment?
(2) Which current authority governs the adjustment terms for the advance in the case being studied? If none is located, mark the point unresolved.
(3) Under 7th CPC, if both lodging and boarding are free on tour, what percentage of Daily Allowance is payable?
(4) How is the Daily Allowance (Food component) calculated on the day of departure for an overnight journey starting at 21:30?
(5) When is CTG payable for a transfer within the same city, and at what rate?
(6) Who bears the TA cost when an employee goes on deputation and subsequently returns to their parent department?
(7) What is the maximum age limit for a dependent son to be eligible for retirement TA? What if the son is disabled?
(8) What primary source, scope, and conditions support any disability-related local-taxi claim for official tour versus LTC?

Rapid-Revision Recap — Chapter 11

  • TA advances: Do not apply a general 90% tour/transfer cap based on the inspected material. A 15-day tour-advance adjustment appears in the 2005 Compendium copy (current currency unverified); no general 30-day transfer deadline is established here.
  • Ordinary tour/transfer/training TA claim submission is due within 60 days whether or not an advance was drawn. Do not infer an advance-adjustment deadline from GFR Rule 290.
  • Free meals + stay on tour reduces Hotel and Food DA components to NIL. No flat 25% allowance exists anymore under 7th CPC. Only actual local travel is reimbursable.
  • Overnight journey DA is calendar-day-wise (midnight-to-midnight): <6h = 30%, 6-12h = 70%, >12h = 100% of the daily lump-sum food ceiling.
  • Departmental exams/LDCE give tour TA but no DA for halts at the exam station, and is capped at 2 attempts.
  • Same-city transfer CTG is Nil unless residence changes, which allows a reduced 1/3 CTG (26.67% of Basic Pay).
  • Deputation TA (both onward and reversion) is borne and paid entirely by the borrowing department.
  • Probationers get Nil TA on first joining.
  • Retirement TA to an alternate settlement place is capped at the declared home-town distance and fare. Family of a retiree who dies before the journey remains fully eligible within 1 year of retirement.
  • Disability-related TA: the 17.02.2015 DoE O.M. provides a conditional escort-fare route, not an escort DA entitlement. A general local AC-taxi exemption for official tour/training and a 500 kg transfer/retirement baggage supplement were not established by the primary sources checked. Keep both claims unverified; the separate LTC own/hired-taxi exception has its own conditions.
  • SR 172: Non-Government candidates called for UPSC/SSC interviews get Second Class train fare if distance >80 km (SC/ST exempt from 80 km minimum). No DA for halts.
  • Transfer vs Tour: Apply the transfer order and current SR 114 conditions; do not infer category or CTG solely from duration.
  • TA bill revision: Supplementary/revised claims admissible within 3 years of original claim.
Appendix A

Flashcard / Active-Recall Bank

Appendix study mapRECALL PROMPTS
FocusUse flashcards for retrieval after studying the source card.
Source reviewFlashcard answers are not a substitute for source verification; the bank has not been validated as a complete answer key.
Study moveMark each card “know / unsure / source check” and revisit uncertain items.
Master TA O.M. number and date?19030/1/2017-E.IV, dated 13.07.2017 (effective 01.07.2017).
Which O.M. did it supersede?19030/3/2008-E.IV dated 23.09.2008.
4 kinds of TA?Tour, Transfer, Retirement, Mileage/Fixed TA.
Ordinary TA claim-submission time limit?For tour/transfer/training, 60 days succeeding completion of journey under GFR Rule 290 and DoE O.M. 13.03.2018, whether or not an advance was drawn. This does not set an advance-adjustment deadline; retirement TA has a separate 180-day order.
What advance-adjustment deadlines were located?The inspected 2005 Compendium Rule 51 says 15 days for a tour advance (currency unverified). Rule 43 for transfer advances gives no general 30-day deadline in the inspected copy. Verify current instructions.
Penal interest rate on overdue/unadjusted TA advances?2% per annum over the GPF interest rate, calculated from the date of drawal to the date of recovery.
Lump-sum recovery rule for overdue TA advances?The entire outstanding advance is recovered in a lump sum from the employee's salary of the immediate next month.
Retirement TA claim time-limit?180 days succeeding the date of completion of the journey (clarified in O.M. dated 15.06.2021).
Level 14+ travel entitlement?Business/Club Class air; AC-I train.
Which levels first got air entitlement (economy) under 7th CPC?Levels 6 to 11 (a major liberalisation vs 6th CPC).
Level 5 and below — air entitlement?None (ground travel only — AC-III/Chair Car by train).
3 components of Daily Allowance on tour?Hotel stay reimbursement + Travel-within-city (local taxi/auto) + Food (lump sum, no voucher).
Self-certification threshold for Hotel/Travel DA?Levels 8 and below may use the stated route up to the applicable ceiling; the Class-X ₹1,000/day hotel ceiling is voucher-backed. The interaction for claims within the ordinary ceiling is unresolved here.
Daily Allowance (Food component) timing scale?<6h = 30%; 6–12h = 70%; >12h = 100% of daily lump sum.
Daily Allowance reduction rule if both free boarding and lodging are provided?Hotel and Food DA components are reduced to NIL. No flat 25% DA is payable. Only actual local travel expenses under Para 2E are admissible.
What 8 km test does the signed local-journey clarification use?DoE letter No. 904302/2018-E.IV dated 12.09.2018 says the temporary duty point must be beyond 8 km from the normal place of duty, whether travel starts from residence or that duty point. It does not settle all fares/vehicle treatment at or below 8 km; see Chapter 3.
Is there an age limit for a dependent son to be eligible for TA/LTC?Yes, up to 25 years (or until marriage/earning, whichever is earliest). However, there is no age limit for disabled sons.
What is the dependency income ceiling for family members under 7th CPC?Monthly income from all sources must not exceed ₹9,000/- plus Dearness Relief (DR) thereon.
Name the 8 Class-X cities under HRA/TA classification.Delhi, Mumbai, Kolkata, Chennai, Bengaluru, Hyderabad, Ahmedabad, Pune.
What are the three ATAs for Government-account air-ticket booking?IRCTC, Balmer Lawrie & Company Limited (BLCL), and Ashok Travels & Tours (ATT). Do not extend the air-ticket rule to all rail bookings.
Is AITTSL an authorized travel agent for Government-account air tickets?No. Use the three ATAs for air tickets under the applicable orders; an airline’s identity as carrier does not itself authorize direct booking. An unavoidable exception needs an authorized relaxation.
What happens if a Government-account air ticket is booked outside the required channel?The fare may be disallowed unless an authorized relaxation is granted for unavoidable circumstances under the applicable order. Check the exact order and competent authority; do not treat this as a rule for every rail booking.
Are cancellation charges reimbursable?Yes, if the journey is cancelled/postponed strictly in public interest, supported by a Controlling Officer's certificate.
How is TA regulated if a longer/circuitous route is taken for personal convenience?TA is strictly restricted to the fare of the shortest route, and extra travel time is treated as leave (not duty).
What are the steamer travel cabin-class entitlements for SCI island ships?PML 9+: Deluxe; PML 6-8: First Class / 'A' Cabin; PML 4-5: Second Class / 'B' Cabin; PML 1-3: Bunk Class.
How is training DA calculated where boarding/lodging are provided, free, or compulsory at fixed rates?No universal formula is verified. Secondary SR 164 transcriptions describe facility-specific schedules and a distinct fixed-rate special allowance; DoE O.M. 01.02.2018 separately addresses free boarding/lodging. Verify the current order and facts before calculating.
Default own-car mileage rate (Current)?₹30/km (applicable today since DA ≥ 50%).
Default auto/scooter mileage (Current)?₹15/km (applicable today since DA ≥ 50%).
CTG rate for transfer ≥20 km?80% of last month's basic pay.
CTG rate for A&N/Lakshadweep transfer?100% of last month's basic pay.
CTG rate for transfer within the same city/station?Nil if no change of residence; one-third (1/3) of the normal rate (26.67% of Basic Pay) if there is an actual change of residence.
Who bears the TA cost for an employee on deputation?The borrowing department/organization bears and pays the Transfer TA for both the onward journey (joining) and the return journey (reversion).
Does a direct-recruit probationer get transfer TA on first joining?No, first-time joining does not qualify for any Transfer TA (under SR 116).
Does a serving employee get DA for halts while appearing in a departmental exam (LDCE)?No, under SR 113, no Daily Allowance is admissible for halts at the exam center. Only travel fare and transit DA are paid.
What caps a retiree's TA if they settle at a place other than home town?Reimbursement is strictly capped at the notional TA/fare to their declared home town, or actual expenditure, whichever is less.
What happens to retirement TA if the employee dies after retirement but before performing the journey?The eligible family members retain the full entitlement to perform the journey and claim TA within the original 1-year window.
Is pay-level-independent local AC taxi reimbursement for a Divyang employee on official tour verified here?No. The primary sources checked establish ordinary level-based tour-local-travel rules, a 2020 voucher procedure change for Levels 9–11, and conditional escort fare; they do not establish a general disability-specific local AC-taxi exemption. Keep any separate signed order on file before relying on one.
Is an extra 500 kg baggage allowance for assistive devices on transfer/retirement verified?No current Central primary source for that supplement was located in this review. Do not treat the claim as an established entitlement pending the operative signed order.
Current Level 14+ hotel ceiling?₹9,375/day (applicable today).
Current Gratuity ceiling?₹25 lakh (up from ₹20 lakh due to DA crossing 50%).
Attendant/Escort TA O.M. date?17.02.2015 (O.M. 19030/3/2013-E.IV).
Tejas Express admissibility clarified when?12.09.2022.
Death-in-service family TA advance cap?75% of the likely TA claim.
O.M. date for "vouchers for travel-within-city DA component"?22.12.2020 (O.M. 19030/2/2020-E.IV).
O.M. date for "Boarding Pass submission with TA bills"?Originally 08.10.2014 (O.M. 19030/3/2014-E.IV), reiterated 23.06.2020.
What does DoPT F.No. G-14019/02/2026-Cash dated 16.07.2026 cover?A Government-hosted DGT reproduction was retrieved. It is a DoPT Cash Section circular addressed to DoPT officers/officials. It reiterates the LTC-air 21-day booking encouragement and at-least-30-day advance request for air-entitled employees under the 29.08.2022 instructions, with the specified authorized-travel-agency webpage printout showing suitable flight/fare details. The 21 days are not a compulsory claim deadline. The circular is limited to DoPT staff and LTC air; it does not create a general TA or rail-booking rule. A direct DoPT-hosted copy was not obtained in this pass; the cited PDF is hosted by DGT.
Is a Pay Level 7 officer entitled to travel by air (Economy) on tour? What about on LTC?On Tour: Yes, fully entitled to Air (Economy Class). On LTC: No, restricted to Rail (AC-II Tier), except under special dispensation schemes.
Who is responsible for paying Joining Time Pay under Rule 7 of CCS (Joining Time) Rules, 1979?It is paid for strictly by the new administrative authority (the borrowing department / new organization) where the Government servant joins on transfer.
Is Daily Allowance (DA) admissible during Special Casual Leave (SCL) due to curfews or disturbances on tour?Yes, fully admissible for the entire period of such SCL (SR 51, GIO (4)(b)).
What Food DA does the signed DoE local-journey letter state?For qualifying journeys beyond 8 km from the normal place of duty, it states 50% of the tour food amount: 15% for <6 hours, 35% for 6–12 hours, and 50% for >12 hours. See letter No. 904302/2018-E.IV dated 12.09.2018; full current SR 71/GIO remains open.
Does the signed letter establish a taxi ban for a journey under 8 km from residence?No such residence-based ban is established by the letter. It measures the qualifying local-journey threshold from the normal place of duty, regardless of origin. It does not decide public fares or taxi/auto/own-car reimbursement at or below 8 km; keep those claims open pending the current signed SR/GIO.
Does DA automatically reset when theory and practical training are held at different stations?No general reset is verified. Secondary transcriptions of P&T 1982/1984 orders describe fresh-course treatment at the practical-training station for a limited in-service/refresher setting; the original letters, current status, and applicability to other courses/cadres remain unverified. Do not apply a new 180-day limit at every station change; see Chapter 11.
Is transportation of a personal car on transfer reimbursable if the car is registered in the spouse's name?No strictly. Transport of personal conveyance is reimbursable ONLY if it is registered strictly in the name of the Government servant themselves.
What is the CTG split if both husband and wife are transferred from the same old station to the same new station within 6 months?If transfers are ordered more than 60 days apart: earlier spouse gets full CTG (80% of Basic Pay), later spouse gets half CTG (40% of Basic Pay). If ordered within 60 days of each other: later spouse gets Nil (Zero).
If both husband and wife are transferred separately, can they claim transport for both of their personal cars?Yes, provided that both cars are registered separately in their respective individual names.
On retirement, if both spouses are Central Government servants and choose to settle at different stations, can they claim TA separately?Yes, fully. They can claim Travelling Allowance for their respective journeys and personal effects separately, up to their respective individual entitlements.
Can husband and wife (both Central Government servants) declare separate Home Towns under LTC rules?Yes, they are allowed to declare separate Home Towns and claim LTC separately under the normal provisions.
Is the family of a deceased temporary employee with 2 years of continuous service eligible for death-in-service TA concessions?No. Temporary employees must have rendered at least three (3) years of regular continuous service for their family to be eligible (SR 148, GIO (1)).
What security requirement is mandatory to draw a death-in-service family TA advance?The family must provide a surety of a permanent employee of comparable or higher status (SR 148, GIO (3)).
What is the adjustment timeline for a death-in-service family TA advance?Within one month (30 days) of the completion of the journey (or of the last batch's journey if travelling in multiple batches).
What is the Immediate Relief Advance amount given to the family upon the demise of a Government servant while in service?A fixed sum of ₹25,000 (under DoE O.M. No. 12(1)/2016-E.II(A) dated 20.06.2018).
Can the ₹25,000 Immediate Relief Advance on demise be paid in cash?Yes. Under CGA O.M. dated 30.04.2021, DDOs are permitted to disburse this ₹25,000 in cash immediately from Imprest or other sources to prevent delays.
How is the ₹25,000 Immediate Relief Advance on demise adjusted/recovered?It is strictly adjusted and recovered from the first terminal payments (Death Gratuity, GPF balance, leave salary, or pay arrears) due to the deceased employee.
Does a Government servant who quits service by resignation qualify for retirement TA concessions?No strictly. Retirement TA is admissible only on retiring pension, superannuation, invalid, or compensation pension. It is strictly disallowed on resignation, dismissal, or removal.
How does road mileage scale on transfer for road journeys between places not connected by rail?It scales by family count: 1 mileage for self/self+1; 2 mileages if 2 members accompany; 3 mileages if more than 2 accompany (SR 116 GIO (5)).
Are GST, Service Tax, and cess charged on personal effects transport bills reimbursable?Yes, fully reimbursable up to the employee's admissible entitlement limit (SR 116 GIO (10)).
Does the employee have to prove that possessing a vehicle at the old/new station was in the public interest to claim transport costs on transfer?No. Under SR 116, the possession of the conveyance at either station need not be in the public interest to claim reimbursement.
Who bears the Travelling Allowance expenses for an employee's return journey upon reversion from deputation?The borrowing department/organization (the office they are leaving) bears and pays the return Transfer TA.
Who bears the Transfer TA during Foreign Service under SR 206?Both the onward and return Transfer TA are borne and paid entirely by the foreign employer.
Are local conveyance or Daily Allowance reimbursable under LTC?No. Daily Allowance, hotel charges at destination, and local travel/conveyance are not reimbursable under the cited LTC rule.
Can a Government servant travel by own car or hired taxi on normal LTC?Normally no, subject to the specific exceptions. Verify the applicable Divyang provision or unconnected-stretch rule; the latter is limited to a maximum of 100 km each side under the cited order.
What are the LTC claim-submission timelines?Normally, one month after completion of the return journey if an advance was drawn (Rule 15(vi)), and three months if no advance was drawn (Rule 14). Under DoPT O.M. 31011/18/2023-Estt.A-IV dated 21.12.2023, limited relaxation with FA concurrence may extend the no-advance case to six months, or the advance case to three months if the full advance plus penal interest is refunded in one lump sum. See the official DoPT 01.07.2025 FAQ, Q35.
What is the time limit for revision of a TA bill or filing a supplementary claim?Three (3) years from the date of the original claim, as per GFR procedural provisions.
What determines whether a change of duty station is a "Transfer" vs a "Tour"?Use the actual transfer/duty order and the applicable current rule. The official DoE 13.07.2017 O.M. preserves SR 114 admissibility conditions but does not reproduce them; duration alone is not treated here as a verified universal classification test. Check separate CTG, family-travel, baggage and conveyance conditions before deciding.
Is any mileage payable for the first kilometre of a road journey by own car/taxi?No. Under the FR/SR rules (carry-over from 6th CPC), the first kilometre is a "dead kilometre" — no mileage is payable. Calculation begins from the 2nd kilometre onward.
What is the TA entitlement if a transfer involves movement within the same Urban Agglomeration (same city) with a change of residence?A restricted CTG of 1/3rd of the normal rate (26.67% of Basic Pay), plus actual transportation charges for personal effects within the city. If no change of residence, CTG = Nil.
What TA/DA do retired employees (pensioners) get when summoned for a departmental inquiry or court deposition?TA and DA as per their original pay level at the time of retirement for the duration of the visit. This is distinct from re-employment. No LTC is admissible.
What is SR 172 and who does it apply to?SR 172 governs TA for non-Government candidates called for UPSC/SSC written tests or interviews. They get Second Class (Mail/Express) train fare by shortest route, provided the distance exceeds 80 km (SC/ST candidates are exempt from the 80 km minimum). No DA for halts.
How is DA calculated in a "split duty" scenario where an employee leaves HQ, returns mid-day, and goes out again?The total absence from HQ within the single calendar day is aggregated. E.g., 4 hours morning + 4 hours afternoon = 8 hours total → falls in the 6–12 hours band → 70% DA. The two periods are NOT treated as separate journeys.
Can a Railway employee eligible for a Duty Pass claim TA if they choose not to use the Duty Pass?No, unless a specific relaxation is granted by the competent authority. If eligible for a Duty Pass but choosing to travel otherwise, TA for that leg is not admissible.
Which Defence/Armed Forces personnel are governed by PCDA rules instead of the civilian DoE TA Rules?Defence Personnel (Army, Navy, Air Force) and personnel of CISF, Assam Rifles, and other CAPFs are governed by separate PCDA (Pune) orders — not by the civilian DoE O.M. No.19030/1/2017-E.IV.
For an overnight train departing at 8:00 PM and arriving at 2:00 AM next day, what DA is payable for Day 2?Day 2 absence is from midnight to 2:00 AM = 2 hours → falls in the <6 hours band → 30% of the daily food lump sum. Day 2 is NOT treated as a full travel day.
Appendix B

Exam-Format Drill

Appendix study mapUNVERIFIED / HOLD
FocusUse the exam-format questions for discussion and retrieval, not validated scoring.
Source reviewAll 38 packages remain unverified. MCQ-TA-10, -14, -21 and MCQ-TR-09 are unscored with no validated key; every package still needs separate item-by-item review.
Study moveKeep disputed items unscored until their stem, options, key and rationale are checked against primary text.

MCQ Set

Q1. The O.M. No.19030/1/2017-E.IV dated 13.07.2017 is effective from which date?
(a) 13.07.2017   (b) 01.07.2017   (c) 01.01.2017   (d) 01.04.2017
Answer: (b) — the O.M. is explicitly backdated to 01.07.2017; (a) is a trap using the issue date instead of the effective date; (c) and (d) are generic fiscal-year-start distractors with no textual basis.

Q2. Under the current (post DA-50%) rates, what is the Mileage Allowance for own car where no state rate is prescribed?
(a) ₹24/km (2017 base)   (b) ₹27/km   (c) ₹30/km   (d) ₹36/km
Answer: (c) — ₹30/km is the currently applicable rate since DA ≥ 50%; (a) is the 2017 base rate (₹24/km); (b) and (d) are arithmetic distractors.

Q3. For a station-to-station transfer of 18 km with no actual change of residence, Composite Transfer Grant is:
(a) 100% of basic pay   (b) 80% of basic pay   (c) Nil   (d) 50% of basic pay
Answer: (c) — 18 km is below the 20 km threshold and the stem specifies no actual change of residence, so CTG is nil; (b) is the trap for candidates who forget the 20 km cutoff.

Q4. Was a fresh Office Memorandum required to raise TA/DA rates by 25% when DA crossed 50% in January 2024?
(a) Yes, O.M. dated 01.01.2024   (b) Yes, O.M. dated 13.07.2024   (c) No — automatic under the 2017 O.M.'s standing clause   (d) No revision occurred
Answer: (c) — this is the single most important nuance of Chapter 8; (a) and (b) invent plausible-sounding but non-existent dedicated TA O.Ms; (d) is factually wrong.

Q5 (Assertion-Reason). Assertion (A): Levels 6–8 officials cannot travel by air on tour. Reason (R): Only Level 9 and above were given air entitlement under the 7th CPC.
(a) Both A and R are true, R explains A   (b) Both A and R are true, R does not explain A   (c) A is false, R is false   (d) A is true, R is false
Answer: (c) — both statements are false: Levels 6–11 (not just 9+) are entitled to Economy air travel under 7th CPC.

Q6 (Statement-based). Consider the following statements about DA on tour: (1) Food component requires a voucher. (2) Hotel stays in Class-X cities always need a voucher for Level ≤8. (3) DA is reckoned midnight-to-midnight. How many statements are correct?
(a) Only one   (b) Only two   (c) All three   (d) None
No validated key — unscored. Statement (1) is false: the food component is a lump sum, not a separate voucher claim. Statement (3) is supported: absence from Headquarters is reckoned midnight-to-midnight on a per-day basis. Statement (2) remains unresolved: the 13.07.2017 DoE O.M. allows Level 8 and below to self-certify hotel claims up to the ceiling, while separately setting a Class-X ceiling of ₹1,000/day against relevant vouchers. The exact interaction for a Level 8-or-below Class-X claim within the ordinary ceiling is not established by this targeted review. The source-snapshot key (b) is withdrawn; do not score this item until the applicable instruction and voucher scope are confirmed.

Q7. If an employee draws a Tour TA Advance and returns to headquarters on 10th July 2026, the final adjustment bill must be submitted on or before:
(a) 25th July 2026   (b) 10th August 2026   (c) 10th September 2026   (d) 31st July 2026
Correction: This question cannot be treated as a current-rule answer key on the reviewed sources. GFR Rules 224/290 do not establish the asserted advance adjustment rule. The inspected 2005 Compendium Rule 51 says 15 days for tour advances, but its currency is unverified; obtain the current departmental instruction before calculating a deadline.

Q8. A Level 8 employee is transferred within the same city, which involves an actual change of residence. Their entitlement for Composite Transfer Grant (CTG) is:
(a) Nil   (b) 80% of Basic Pay   (c) 1/3 of the normal rate (26.67% of Basic Pay)   (d) 50% of Basic Pay
Answer: (c) — For same-station transfers involving change of residence, the CTG is restricted to 1/3 of the normal rate (which is 1/3 of 80% = 26.67%). If there was no change of residence, it would be Nil (Option a).

Q9. Which department bears the Travelling Allowance expenses for the onward and return journeys of a Government servant going on deputation?
(a) Onward by Lending Department, Return by Borrowing Department
(b) Both Onward and Return by Borrowing Department
(c) Both Onward and Return by Lending Department
(d) Onward by Borrowing Department, Return by Lending Department
Verification status: UNVERIFIED — do not score; no key assigned. The proposed key (b) is withdrawn. The DoPT consolidated deputation guidance surfaced in the official record says Travelling Allowance and Transfer TA are regulated by mutual consent of the lending and borrowing organizations; it does not establish a universal borrowing-organization-only rule. Check the specific deputation terms and applicable instructions before deciding who bears each leg.

Q10. A Level 9 Divyang (disabled) employee travels on official tour. Regarding local conveyance within the tour city, they are entitled to:
(a) Non-AC taxi / auto charges up to ₹423 per day (under normal Level 9 ceiling)
(b) Only public bus fare reimbursement
(c) AC Taxi/hired taxi charges as per actuals/official engagements, irrespective of their normal Level 9 ceiling
(d) No local conveyance reimbursement of any kind
Verification status: UNVERIFIED — do not score; no key assigned. The proposed key (c) and its categorical rationale are withdrawn. The official 2017 DoE O.M. sets pay-level local-travel entitlements; the 22.12.2020 O.M. changes receipts/self-certification for Levels 9–11, not taxi class or ceiling. The 17.02.2015 escort O.M. does not establish an unrestricted local-AC-taxi benefit for the employee. The separate DoPT disability-related taxi exception located is limited to LTC and has conditions. Seek a specific applicable Central primary order before restoring any key.

Q11. A retiring Level 10 officer settles in a city 1,500 km away from their last headquarters, while their declared home town is only 600 km away. Their retirement TA (fares and transportation) is restricted to:
(a) The actual expenses incurred for the 1,500 km journey
(b) Nil — retirement TA is only allowed when settling in the declared home town
(c) The notional expenses that would have been admissible for the 600 km journey to their home town
(d) 50% of the actual Chennai journey expenses
Verification status: UNVERIFIED — do not score; no key assigned. The proposed key (c) depends on current SR 147 and the applicable retirement-TA instructions, which were not obtained in this review. The guide has not validated the home-town cap, the notional-cost comparison, or how the stated fares and personal-effects components apply to these facts.

Q12. A Pay Level 7 employee is planning their travel. Under the 7th CPC rules, what is their air travel (Economy Class) entitlement on official tour versus Leave Travel Concession (LTC)?
(a) Entitled to Air travel on both Tour and LTC
(b) Entitled to Air travel on Tour, but restricted to AC-II Tier Rail on LTC
(c) Entitled to Air travel on LTC, but restricted to Rail on Tour
(d) Not entitled to Air travel on either Tour or LTC
Answer: (b) — Under DoPT O.M. dated 19.09.2017, the guide’s stated contrast is that Pay Levels 6–8 may have economy-air entitlement for official tour/transfer but have a different normal LTC mode entitlement, subject to special-dispensation or later orders. This MCQ’s key and rationale have not been independently verified as part of the full bank audit; do not score it as verified.

Q13. Under the LTC rules, if a Government servant travels by private taxi on a stretch not connected by any public transport, what is the maximum distance for which they can claim reimbursement?
(a) 50 km each side
(b) 100 km each side (to-and-fro combined 200 km)
(c) No limit, actual distance is reimbursed
(d) Private taxi is completely prohibited under all circumstances on LTC
Answer: (b), subject to the order’s scope. DoPT O.M. 31011/3/2015-Estt.(A-IV), 04.02.2021, clarifies that the 100-km cap in the unconnected-stretch LTC route is each side (200 km to-and-fro). The underlying exception is conditional: the journey is to the declared LTC place after using authorized transport to the nearest airport/railway station/bus terminal, no public transport is available on the relevant stretch, and the prescribed self-certification and entitled-transfer-journey rate cap apply. This targeted source check does not verify the whole MCQ package.

Q14. If a Central Government employee undergoes a training programme divided into theoretical training at Station A and practical training at Station B, how is the continuous halt limit of 180 days for Daily Allowance calculated?
(a) The 180 days limit continues continuously across both stations combined
(b) The 180 days limit commences afresh at Station B, regardless of the days of DA drawn at Station A
(c) No DA is allowed at either station since it is a training programme
(d) DA is restricted to 30 days only for both stations combined
Verification status: UNVERIFIED — do not score or assign a key pending primary-source review. The previous key (b) is withdrawn. Secondary transcriptions of P&T 1982/1984 orders describe a fresh-course treatment at the practical-training station in a limited in-service/refresher setting; the original letters, current status, and applicability to this general Central Government question were not verified. This item does not establish a universal 180-day reset.

Q15. To claim transportation charges of a personal motor car on transfer, what is the strict ownership requirement for registration under SR 116?
(a) The car can be registered in the name of the officer or their spouse
(b) The car must be registered strictly in the name of the Government servant themselves
(c) The car can be registered in the name of any dependent family member
(d) There is no registration or ownership requirement, only possession must be shown
Verification status: UNVERIFIED — do not score; no key assigned. The proposed key (b) and the categorical “only in the employee’s name” rationale are not validated against the current complete SR 116 text. Check the operative rule and any applicable DoE clarification before deciding whether spouse/dependent registration disqualifies the claim.

Q16. Which of the following temporary employees' families is eligible to draw Travelling Allowance concessions on Death-in-Service under SR 148?
(a) Family of a temporary employee with 1 year of continuous service
(b) Family of a temporary employee with 2 years of continuous service
(c) Family of a temporary employee with 3 years or more of regular continuous service
(d) Families of temporary employees are completely excluded, regardless of service length
Verification status: UNVERIFIED — do not score; no key assigned. The proposed key (c) and the three-year eligibility condition have not been checked against the current complete SR 148/GIO text. Verify the exact employee category, service definition, threshold and benefit scope before scoring.

Q17. An officer is posted from Delhi to Gurugram (within the same Urban Agglomeration) for an indefinite period, and changes residence. Their Composite Transfer Grant (CTG) entitlement is:
(a) Nil — same city, no TA
(b) Full CTG (80% of Basic Pay) — it is a permanent transfer
(c) 1/3rd of normal CTG (26.67% of Basic Pay) — same UA with change of residence
(d) 50% of normal CTG
Answer: (c) — A transfer within the same Urban Agglomeration involving an actual change of residence attracts a restricted CTG of 1/3rd of the normal rate (1/3 × 80% = 26.67% of Basic Pay). Option (a) would apply only if there was no change of residence. Option (b) applies only for inter-station transfers ≥20 km.

Q18. A Level 7 officer travels 12 km by own car for an official tour in an area with no state-notified rate. The Mileage Allowance payable is:
(a) ₹360 (12 × ₹30)
(b) ₹330 (11 × ₹30)
(c) ₹180 (12 × ₹15)
(d) ₹165 (11 × ₹15)
Verification status: UNVERIFIED — do not score; no key assigned. The own-car rate component (₹30/km under the checked rate update) is separate from the asserted “first kilometre not payable” rule. The current Central primary authority and applicability of the dead-kilometre deduction were not established in this review; do not calculate 11 × ₹30 without that source.

Q19. Under SR 172, a non-Government candidate (General category) called for a UPSC interview is entitled to travel fare reimbursement only if the one-way distance from their residence to the venue exceeds:
(a) 25 km
(b) 50 km
(c) 80 km
(d) 100 km
Verification status: UNVERIFIED — do not score; no key assigned. The current SR 172 text and applicable UPSC/recruiting-body instruction were not retrieved. The 80-km threshold, distance measurement and asserted SC/ST exception therefore remain unverified.

Q20. A TA bill was originally passed and paid in September 2022. In October 2025, the Accounts section discovers an arithmetic error and wishes to revise the bill. Is this revision admissible?
(a) Yes — within the 3-year revision window
(b) No — the 60-day claim limit has expired
(c) No — the 3-year revision window has expired (September 2022 to October 2025 = over 3 years)
(d) Yes — TA bills can be revised at any time
Verification status: UNVERIFIED — do not score; no key assigned. No current applicable Central authority was located for the asserted three-year revision period or its starting date. The 60-day initial claim-submission rule does not by itself establish a bill-revision limitation. Verify the governing correction/revision instruction and trigger before answering.

Q21. An officer is sent on temporary duty to another station for a period of 5 months (150 days). Which of the following statements is correct?
(a) This is a Transfer — full CTG and baggage transport are admissible
(b) This is a Tour — only travel fare and Daily Allowance are admissible; no CTG
(c) This is a Transfer because 150 days exceeds 120 days
(d) This qualifies for 50% CTG as a semi-permanent posting
Verification status: UNRESOLVED — do not score; no answer key assigned. The earlier proposed answer (b) and its duration-only rationale are withdrawn. The official DoE 13.07.2017 O.M. confirms that SR 114 general admissibility conditions continue to apply to transfer TA, but does not reproduce the current SR 114 text; the governing order conditions and any relevant amendments remain unverified. Duration alone does not settle this item. See Chapter 5’s source-status note.

Withdrawn draft rationale (retained for audit trail; not a key)

The earlier draft treated “under 180 days” as sufficient to classify the case as a Tour with no CTG or baggage transport. That conclusion is not validated here. It must not be used as a rule or scoring key.

Q22. A retired Level 12 officer is summoned by a CBI court to depose as a witness in a corruption case. For the 3-day visit, they are entitled to:
(a) No TA — they are no longer a Government employee
(b) TA/DA as per Level 12 (their retirement pay level), including travel fare and Daily Allowance
(c) TA/DA as per Level 1 (lowest level, since they have no current pay)
(d) Only bus fare reimbursement, no DA
Verification status: UNVERIFIED — do not score; no key assigned. The current FR/SR or specific order governing a retired employee summoned by a CBI court as a witness, including the admissible rate/pay-level basis and DA, was not retrieved. Do not generalize from a different inquiry, medical-board or witness category.

Q23. In a split-duty scenario, an officer leaves HQ at 9:00 AM and returns at 1:00 PM, then leaves again at 3:00 PM and returns at 7:00 PM. Their DA for the day is calculated as:
(a) 30% DA for each trip separately (total 60%)
(b) 70% DA — based on the combined 8-hour absence
(c) 100% DA — since two separate tours were performed
(d) No DA — split duty is not recognized under TA Rules
Verification status: UNVERIFIED — do not score; no key assigned. The current primary rule for aggregating two separated headquarters absences and applying a 70% DA band to this split-duty pattern was not established in this review. Keep this as a discussion prompt pending the exact rule/GIO and source-backed calculation.
