Part II · Technical Resignation & Transfer TA

Technical Resignation, Transfer TA & Joining Pay

Study aid on Technical Resignation, Transfer TA and Joining Time Pay. A targeted audit identified an unresolved interaction between the 2015 Joining Time amendment and DoPT's 2022 consolidation; this section does not determine individual entitlement.

Targeted update · DoPT/CCS Rules · 22 November 2026

Reference notes and practice examples. The contested three-year TTA / Joining Time Pay answers are qualified below; this is not a definitive entitlement guide.

Sources reviewed (not every solution independently verified): This section references DoPT Office Memoranda and CCS Rules, including:
• DoPT O.M. No. DOPT-1669266628362 (NESTS-hosted Government reproduction; direct DoPT record not retrieved) dated 24.11.2022 (Consolidated Instructions on Technical Resignation and Lien)
• CCS (Joining Time) Rules, 1979
• CCS (Leave) Rules, 1972
• FR & SR Part-I & II
• CCS (Pension) Rules, 2021
An official DoPT page for the 2022 record returned an access error during this audit; a Government-hosted reproduction was reviewed. The authority interaction described below remains unresolved.
Important authority conflict — no individual ruling: The 2015 amendment, G.S.R. 229(E) dated 27.03.2015, substituted CCS (Joining Time) Rule 4(4) for qualifying competitive appointments. The substituted wording is conditional (including an option to count past service for all purposes in Central Government) and contains no express three-year cutoff. DoPT O.M. dated 24.11.2022, para 3.6.3, separately states that temporary Central Government employees with less than three years' regular continuous service are not entitled to TTA. Their interaction was not resolved in the targeted audit. Do not state categorically that confirmed employees always qualify or that temporary employees under three years never qualify without checking the operative text, conditions, and any later clarification. Technical-resignation status is a separate question from TTA / Joining Time Pay.
Chapter 12

Fundamentals — What is Technical Resignation?

Chapter study mapPARTIAL / OPEN
FocusApply the technical-resignation conditions without inferring TTA or Joining Time Pay from the label alone.
Source reviewThe 24.11.2022 DoPT consolidation text was reviewed from a Government-hosted NESTS reproduction (identifier DOPT-1669266628362); direct DoPT retrieval returned HTTP 500. Individual benefits are not fully verified.
Study moveCheck application channel, selection and resignation requirement separately; do not treat technical status as an entitlement decision.

Core DefinitionLegal Framework

DoPT O.M. No. DOPT-1669266628362 (NESTS-hosted Government reproduction; direct DoPT record not retrieved) dated 24.11.2022, Para 2.1

As per the Ministry of Finance O.M. No. 3379-E.III(B)/65 dated 17th June 1965, technical resignation is a resignation treated as a technical formality where a Government servant has applied through proper channel for a post in the same or some other Department, and is on selection, required to resign the previous post for administrative reasons.

Critical Point: The resignation will be treated as technical resignation if these conditions are met, even if the Government servant has not mentioned the word "Technical" while submitting his resignation.

Simple Explanation (Hinglish)
  • Technical Resignation kya hai? Maan lo aap ek sarkari naukri mein ho (jaise SSC se select hue, Level 5 pe kaam kar rahe ho). Aapne dusri sarkari naukri ke liye apply kiya — "through proper channel" (matlab apne current office ke through, NOC leke, forwarding letter ke saath). Aap select ho gaye. Ab nayi department mein join karne ke liye purani naukri se resign karna padega. Ye resignation "technical" hai — ye koi voluntary leaving nahi hai, ye sirf ek administrative formality hai taaki aap nayi post join kar sako.
  • "Through Proper Channel" ka matlab: Ye sabse important requirement hai. Aapka application current office se forward hona chahiye — NOC (No Objection Certificate) ke saath, proper forwarding letter ke saath. Agar aapne directly apply kiya bina office ko bataye, to technically ye "proper channel" nahi hai — lekin DoPT ne 2022 ke OM mein ek relaxation di hai (para 2.5): agar authority satisfy ho jaaye ki "agar employee service mein hota to application forward ki jaati," to bhi technical resignation treat ho sakta hai.
  • Word "Technical" likhna zaroori nahi: Agar aapne resignation letter mein "technical resignation" nahi likha — bas normal resign kiya — lekin conditions meet hoti hain (proper channel se apply kiya tha, usi post ke liye resign kar rahe ho), to automatically technical resignation maana jaayega.

Three Essential Conditions for Technical Resignation:

1. Applied through proper channel — Application forwarded by current employer with NOC

2. Selected for a Government post — Central/State Government, PSU, or autonomous body

3. Required to resign for administrative reasons — Not voluntary leaving, but necessity to join new post

NO minimum service requirement for technical resignation itself — it can be done at any stage (even during probation).

Rapid-Revision Recap — Chapter 12: technical-resignation status

  • Identify the application channel, selection for the new post, and whether resignation is required for administrative reasons; record evidence for each.
  • Under the reviewed 24.11.2022 Government-hosted reproduction, technical-resignation treatment is distinct from Joining Time Pay and TTA. The direct DoPT-hosted record was not obtained.
  • Do not infer TTA, Joining Time Pay, lien, pay protection or leave treatment from the phrase “technical resignation” alone.
  • For an individual case, verify the operative order, employment category, destination, dates and competent-authority decision.
Chapter 13

Joining Time & TTA — 2015 Amendment and 2022 Consolidation

Chapter study mapUNRESOLVED
FocusRead 2015 Rule 4(4) and 2022 paras 3.6.1–3.6.3 separately.
Source reviewThe Official Gazette 27.03.2015 text was fetched and confirms the substituted Rule 4(4) conditions. The 2022 DoPT text was reviewed from a Government-hosted reproduction; the interaction for temporary Central Government employees under three years and TTA is not reconciled here.
Study moveDo not memorize a categorical three-year answer; check all conditions and seek a written competent-authority decision.

Authority comparisonJoining Time Pay and TTA — do not use a categorical three-year answer

Unresolved interaction: This replaces the earlier table and flowchart that treated confirmation status or a three-year service threshold as determinative. The audit did not locate an authoritative clarification reconciling the texts for every qualifying case.
AuthorityReviewed provisionSafe conclusion for this guide
CCS (Joining Time) Rules, Rule 4(4), as substituted by G.S.R. 229(E), 27.03.2015Provides for qualifying competitive appointments, subject to stated conditions including an option to count past service for all purposes in Central Government; the substituted wording contains no express three-year cutoff.Check whether the appointment and all conditions fall within the substituted rule; do not infer coverage from service length alone.
DoPT O.M. No. DOPT-1669266628362 (NESTS-hosted Government reproduction; direct DoPT record not retrieved), 24.11.2022, para 3.6.3States that temporary Central Government employees with less than three years of regular continuous service are not entitled to TTA.Read with the 2015 amendment and any subsequent clarification; the interaction is not settled by this guide.
Technical resignation statusDoPT technical-resignation provisions concern whether a resignation is treated as technical and related service continuity.It does not itself decide entitlement to TTA or Joining Time Pay.

Use this decision aid instead of the old yes/no chart:

1. First assess technical-resignation status under the applicable DoPT conditions (including proper-channel / prior-application rules where relevant).

2. Separately check whether the appointment is within Rule 4(4) as substituted in 2015, including its conditions and service-counting option.

3. Separately read DoPT O.M. para 3.6.3 and check for later binding clarification or department-specific instructions.

4. If the two provisions affect the case, obtain a written decision from the competent authority. This study aid does not decide an employee's TTA or Joining Time Pay.

Simple Terms (Hinglish)
  • Technical resignation aur TTA/Joining Time Pay alag sawal hain. Technical resignation valid ho sakta hai, lekin usse TTA ya joining-time pay automatically decide nahi hota.
  • 2015 ke Rule 4(4) amendment mein qualifying competitive appointments ke liye conditions hain, including past service ko Central Government mein all purposes ke liye count karne ka option; us substituted wording mein explicit 3-year cutoff nahi dikhta.
  • DoPT ke 24.11.2022 OM, para 3.6.3, temporary Central Government employees with under 3 years ke TTA ko restrict karta hai.
  • In dono texts ka interaction yahan unresolved hai. Sirf "confirmed" ya "temporary + under 3 years" se final yes/no mat nikaalo; competent authority se applicable rule aur written decision lo.

Rapid-Revision Recap — Chapter 13: keep both authorities visible

  • The 2015 Gazette substitutes CCS (Joining Time) Rule 4(4) with conditions for qualifying competitive appointments, including the option concerning past service; its substituted text contains no express three-year cutoff.
  • DoPT’s 24.11.2022 consolidation para 3.6.3 separately states a three-year condition for temporary Central Government employees’ TTA through Joining Time Pay.
  • Interaction unresolved: do not infer a categorical answer from confirmation, probation or years served alone; ask the competent authority to apply both texts to the actual facts.
  • Keep the Official Gazette text distinct from the Government-hosted NESTS reproduction; the direct DoPT record was not retrieved. This recap is not an individual ruling.
Chapter 14

15 Case Studies — Facts, Conditions & Escalation (Not Rulings)

Chapter study mapCASE STUDIES / NOT RULINGS
FocusUse case studies to identify facts, applicable rules, missing information and escalation points.
Source reviewThe scenarios are not claimant-specific decisions and were not individually validated against every source/fact pattern.
Study moveFor each case, separate stated facts from assumptions and list what must be verified before deciding.

Core QuestionCase 1 — Confirmed Employee, 2.5 Years Service

Facts: Mr. Sharma joined as Section Officer (Level 8) through UPSC CSS Exam in July 2022. His probation period was 2 years. He was confirmed in July 2024 (confirmation order issued). In January 2025, he was selected for the post of Deputy Secretary (Level 11) through a competitive interview open to Government servants. He submitted technical resignation to join the new post. Total service = 2 years 6 months (confirmed).

Question: Is Mr. Sharma eligible for Transfer Travelling Allowance (TTA) and Joining Time Pay on technical resignation?

⚠️ No TTA / Joining Time Pay conclusion is made here.

Technical-resignation status may be assessed separately under DoPT's conditions. For TTA / Joining Time Pay, these facts raise the unresolved interaction between substituted Rule 4(4) (2015) and DoPT O.M. para 3.6.3 (2022). Confirmation and 2.5 years' service do not, by themselves, resolve that issue in this guide. Check the rule conditions and any later clarification; obtain a written decision from the competent authority.

Probation ScenarioCase 2 — Probationer, 1.5 Years Service (Not Yet Confirmed)

Facts: Ms. Patel joined as Inspector (Level 6) through SSC CGL in March 2023. Her probation period is 2 years (until March 2025). In September 2024 (1.5 years of service, still on probation), she was selected for a post in another Central Government department through proper channel. She submitted technical resignation.

Question: Is Ms. Patel eligible for TTA and Joining Time Pay?

⚠️ No TTA / Joining Time Pay conclusion is made here.

The 2022 DoPT consolidation states a restriction for temporary Central Government employees with less than three years' regular continuous service. The 2015 substituted Rule 4(4) may also be relevant if this is a qualifying competitive appointment and its conditions are met. The interaction was not resolved in this audit; the facts given do not support a categorical yes/no answer here. Technical-resignation status is separate.

Threshold CaseCase 3 — Temporary Employee with Exactly 3 Years Service

Facts: Mr. Kumar joined as LDC (Level 2) on ad-hoc basis in January 2021. His service was regularized in June 2021 (after departmental process). He has not been confirmed (no probation prescribed for this post, but also no confirmation order issued — service is "temporary" in nature). In June 2024, he was selected for another post through proper channel. Total regular continuous service = exactly 3 years (June 2021 to June 2024, counting from regularization).

Question: Is Mr. Kumar eligible for TTA and Joining Time Pay?

⚠️ This example does not decide TTA / Joining Time Pay.

DoPT O.M. para 3.6.3 and the 2015 substituted Rule 4(4) must be checked against the actual appointment, service record, and conditions. The guide does not make an individual entitlement ruling from the three-year threshold alone.

Lien ScenarioCase 4 — Confirmed Employee Joining State Government Post

Facts: Mr. Singh is a confirmed Section Officer (Level 8, Central Government) with 4 years of service. He is selected for a post in a State Government through proper channel. He submits technical resignation.

Question: Does he get TTA? Can he retain lien?

⚠️ TTA is not decided here. The lien statement is a separate topic and remains subject to the applicable DoPT conditions.

Reasoning:

  • TTA: No individual entitlement decision is made here. Check the applicable 2015 Rule 4(4) conditions and DoPT O.M. para 3.6.3, including any later clarification.
  • Lien (Para 3.1): A permanent Government servant appointed in another Central Government Department/State Government retains lien on parent post for 2 years. He must revert within 2 years (or 3 years in exceptional cases — e.g., if not confirmed in new department within 2 years). An undertaking to this effect is taken at the time of forwarding his application.
  • Important — Lien will cease if: He does not revert within 2/3 years, OR he is permanently absorbed in the State Government. In that case, lien terminates from the date of absorption.

Applied Before JoiningCase 5 — Employee Applied for Another Post BEFORE Joining Current Service

Facts: Ms. Gupta applied for UPSC IAS exam in February 2024 (while in private sector). She joined as Section Officer (Central Govt) through SSC in April 2024. In August 2024, she got selected for IAS and was asked to resign from the SO post. She had NOT applied "through proper channel" since the application was made before joining.

Question: Is this a valid technical resignation? Does she get TTA?

⚠️ CONDITIONAL — This may be treated as technical resignation if DoPT O.M. para 2.5 conditions are met. TTA / Joining Time Pay are separate and not decided here.

Reasoning:

  • DoPT O.M. Para 2.5 (special provision): This benefit is specifically available to Government servants "who have applied before joining the Government service and on that account the application was not routed through proper channel." Conditions:
    (i) The Government servant should intimate the details of such application immediately on joining.
    (ii) At the time of resignation, should specifically request that the resignation be treated as technical resignation.
    (iii) The authority should satisfy itself that "had the employee been in service on the date of application, the application would have been forwarded through proper channel."
  • TTA / Joining Time Pay: Not determined by this example. Check whether the competitive appointment falls within substituted Rule 4(4) and its conditions, read with DoPT O.M. para 3.6.3; obtain a written decision. Do not infer entitlement or non-entitlement from confirmation status alone.

Common TrapCase 6 — Direct Resignation Without Proper Channel

Facts: Mr. Verma is a confirmed Level 7 officer (4 years service). He applied for another department's post directly (without informing his current office, without NOC, without forwarding through proper channel). He got selected and submitted a plain resignation to join the new post. He did NOT intimate his current office about the application.

Question: Is this a technical resignation? Does he get TTA and past service benefits?

❌ NO — This is NOT a technical resignation. He gets NO TTA and NO past service benefits.

Reasoning:

  • DoPT O.M. Para 2.1: "Resignation in other cases including where competent authority has not allowed the Government servant to forward the application through proper channel will not be treated as a technical resignation and benefit of past service will not be admissible."
  • Consequences:
    • ❌ NO leave carry-forward (EL/HPL/CCL lapses)
    • ❌ NO LTC continuity (treated as fresh recruit in new department)
    • ❌ NO pay protection
    • ❌ NO past service counted for increment
    • ❌ NO pension continuity (if pre-2004 appointee)
    • ❌ NO TTA (since it's not a technical resignation, joining time rules don't apply in the same way)
    • ❌ NO Lien on old post
  • Lesson: ALWAYS apply through proper channel. Even if the process is slow, get the NOC and forwarding letter. This single step protects ALL your service benefits.

Pension ScenarioCase 7 — Pre-2004 Employee (Old Pension Scheme)

Facts: Mr. Iyer joined Central Government service in 2001 (pre-01.01.2004, hence covered under Old Pension Scheme — CCS Pension Rules, 1972). He has 23 years of service (confirmed). He is selected for another Central Government post through proper channel and submits technical resignation.

Question: Will his past 23 years of service count towards pension?

✅ YES — Full 23 years of past service counts towards pension.

Reasoning:

  • DoPT O.M. Para 2.7: "In cases where Government servants, who had originally joined government service prior to 01.01.2004, apply for posts in the same or other Departments and on selection they are asked to tender technical resignation, the past services are counted towards pension under CCS (Pension) Rules, 1972...if the new post is in a pensionable establishment."
  • Continuity of service: "As a consequence, continuity of service benefits should be allowed to such employees in the matter of pension, leave, LTC, etc."
  • Total pensionable service: 23 years (old) + service in new post. At retirement, pension will be calculated on the combined service.

NPS ScenarioCase 8 — Post-2004 Employee (NPS)

Facts: Ms. Reddy joined Central Government service in 2015 (post-01.01.2004, hence covered under National Pension System — NPS). She has 9 years of service (confirmed). She is selected for another Central Government post through proper channel.

Question: What happens to her NPS account on technical resignation?

✅ NPS account (PRAN + balance) is carried forward to the new office.

Reasoning:

  • DoPT O.M. Para 2.8: "In case of Technical Resignation of Government servant covered under National Pension System (NPS), the balance standing to their Personal Retirement Account (PRA) along with their PRAN, will be carried forward to the new office."
  • Process: The old office DDO transfers the PRAN to the new office. Contributions continue uninterrupted. Service continuity is maintained for NPS purposes.
  • No withdrawal: The employee should NOT withdraw from NPS on technical resignation — the account is simply transferred.

Contract StaffCase 9 — Contract Employee Selected for Regular Post

Facts: Mr. Das has been working on a contractual basis in a Central Government department for 4 years. He is selected for a regular Central Government post through proper channel. He resigns from the contract post to join the regular post.

Question: Is this a technical resignation? Does he get TTA?

❌ NO — Contract employees are NOT covered under technical resignation rules.

Reasoning:

  • Contract vs Regular: Technical resignation applies to employees holding regular posts (permanent or temporary). Contract employees are engaged on a fixed-term basis and do not have a "lien" or permanent right to the post.
  • No past service benefits: The contract period does NOT count as "regular service" for purposes of technical resignation. When Mr. Das joins the regular post, he starts fresh — no leave carry-forward, no pay protection, no LTC continuity from the contract period.
  • TTA: Since he was not a regular employee, he does not get TTA. He must bear his own travel expenses to join the new post.

Deputation CaseCase 10 — Employee on Deputation Selected for Another Post

Facts: Ms. Nair is a confirmed Central Government employee (Level 9) currently on deputation to a PSU for 2 years. While on deputation, she applies for and is selected for another Central Government post through proper channel (application forwarded by parent department, not the PSU).

Question: Does she get TTA from the deputation station or parent department station?

✅ YES — She gets TTA, calculated from the deputation station (current place of duty).

Reasoning:

  • Lien remains with parent department: While on deputation, her lien is with the parent department. The technical resignation is from the parent department post.
  • TTA calculation: TTA is calculated based on the distance from her current place of duty (deputation station) to the new post, not from the parent department station. This is because she is physically posted at the deputation station.
  • Deputation period: Her deputation is terminated early (with proper approval) to enable her to join the new post. The PSU releases her, and she joins the new Central Government post.
  • Joining time: Calculated from deputation station to new post.

Same StationCase 11 — New Post in Same City (No Change of Station)

Facts: Mr. Joshi is a confirmed Section Officer in Delhi (Ministry A). He is selected for a Deputy Secretary post in Delhi (Ministry B) through proper channel. Both offices are in Delhi — no change of station.

Question: Does he get TTA? What about joining time?

⚠️ PARTIAL — He gets joining time but limited/no TTA (since no change of station).

Reasoning:

  • Joining Time: He gets joining time (typically 5-7 days for same station transfers as per CCS Joining Time Rules).
  • Joining Time Pay: Yes, he gets joining time pay (full pay + DA for the joining time period).
  • TTA: Since there is no change of station (Delhi to Delhi), no travel is involved. Therefore:
    • No fare reimbursement (no travel needed)
    • No CTG (Composite Transfer Grant) — CTG is for transfer involving change of station
    • No baggage transportation allowance
    • However, if he needs to shift residence within Delhi (different locality), he may claim limited local transportation charges as per TA rules
  • Other benefits: All other benefits (leave carry-forward, pay protection, LTC continuity, lien) apply as normal.

Family Joins LaterCase 12 — Employee Joins Alone, Family Joins Later

Facts: Mr. Mehta (confirmed, Level 10) is transferred from Mumbai to Chennai on technical resignation. He joins alone in January 2025 and takes family later in March 2025 (within permissible time limit).

Question: Does he get full TTA for family? Can he claim joining time for the period when family joins?

✅ YES — He gets full TTA for family (if claimed within time limit). No additional joining time for family's travel.

Reasoning:

  • TTA for family: As per TA Rules, family can join later within the permissible time limit (usually 6 months from date of employee's joining). Mr. Mehta can claim:
    • Family fare (spouse + dependent children) from Mumbai to Chennai
    • CTG (Composite Transfer Grant) — already claimed when he joined
    • Baggage transportation for family's belongings
    • All claims must be submitted within the prescribed time limit
  • Joining time: Mr. Mehta already availed joining time when he joined in January. There is no additional joining time when family joins later. The family travels at their own time (within the 6-month window).
  • Important: Family fare must be claimed within the time limit prescribed under TA Rules (usually within 6 months of family's actual journey).

Multiple ApplicationsCase 13 — Employee Applied for Multiple Posts, Selected for One

Facts: Ms. Khan (confirmed, Level 7) applied for 3 different posts through proper channel: (1) UPSC IAS, (2) SSC CGL (higher post), (3) Departmental promotion. She got selected for the SSC CGL post first and submitted technical resignation.

Question: Is this a valid technical resignation? What about the other applications?

✅ YES — Valid technical resignation for the post she's joining. Other applications become void.

Reasoning:

  • Technical resignation is post-specific: The technical resignation is for the specific post she's joining (SSC CGL post). The fact that she had applied for multiple posts does not invalidate the technical resignation.
  • Other applications: Once she resigns and joins the new post, her other applications (UPSC IAS, departmental promotion) become void. She would need to apply afresh from the new department if she wants to pursue those opportunities.
  • Important: She should inform the new department about any pending applications/results at the time of joining, as this may have implications if she's selected for another post shortly after joining.

Disciplinary CaseCase 14 — Employee Under Disciplinary Proceedings

Facts: Mr. Rao (confirmed, Level 8) is selected for another post through proper channel. However, disciplinary proceedings are pending against him in the current department. The department is reluctant to issue NOC/forward the resignation.

Question: Can he still submit technical resignation? What happens to the disciplinary proceedings?

⚠️ CONDITIONAL — Depends on department's decision. Disciplinary proceedings continue.

Reasoning:

  • Department's discretion: If disciplinary proceedings are pending, the department may refuse to forward the application/resignation. This is at the discretion of the competent authority.
  • If forwarded: If the department does forward the resignation (with or without conditions), it can be treated as technical resignation. The employee gets all benefits.
  • Disciplinary proceedings: The proceedings do NOT automatically get transferred to the new department. The old department can:
    (a) Continue the proceedings and impose penalty (which may affect the employee's record)
    (b) Transfer the proceedings to the new department (with the new department's consent)
    (c) Close the proceedings (if minor)
  • Important: The employee should disclose the pending proceedings to the new department at the time of joining. Concealment may lead to disciplinary action in the new department.

Reversion CaseCase 15 — Employee Reverts to Parent Department After 1 Year

Facts: Ms. Sharma (confirmed, Level 8) submitted technical resignation and joined another Central Government department (Level 9) in January 2024. She retained lien on her old post. In January 2025 (after 1 year), she decides to revert to her parent department.

Question: Can she revert? What happens to her seniority and service in the new department?

✅ YES — She can revert (within lien period). Service in new department counts for some purposes but not for seniority in old department.

Reasoning:

  • Lien valid: She retained lien for 2 years (extendable to 3). Since she's reverting within 1 year, her lien is valid. She can revert to her old post (or equivalent post if old post is not available).
  • Service in new department: As per DoPT O.M. Para 2.4: "in case of a Government servant deciding to rejoin his substantive post, the period spent in another department which he had joined after submitting his technical resignation will not count for minimum qualifying service for promotion in the higher post."
  • What counts:
    • ✅ Service counts for pension (continuous service)
    • ✅ Service counts for leave accrual
    • ✅ Service counts for increment (if pay was protected)
    • ❌ Service does NOT count for seniority in the old department
    • ❌ Service does NOT count for promotion eligibility in the old department
  • Reversion process: She submits a reversion request to the new department, which releases her. She joins the parent department, and her lien is exercised. She resumes her old seniority position.

Rapid-Revision Recap — Chapter 14: case-study method

  • Separate stated facts from assumptions; list the missing fact before choosing an outcome.
  • For technical-resignation/TTA cases, check application channel, selection, resignation requirement, service category, destination, Joining Time condition and the applicable dates.
  • For each benefit, write the source and paragraph independently; one status (e.g., technical resignation) does not prove every related benefit.
  • Label each result conditional / unresolved / supported for this fact pattern; scenarios are study exercises, not rulings.

📝 Practice Questions — Organized by Topic

Practice questions only. The answer keys and rationales have not all been independently verified. In particular, three-year TTA / Joining Time Pay questions below are flagged because the reviewed authorities were not reconciled.

Core RuleQ1. Confirmed Employee TTA Eligibility

Question: A confirmed Central Government employee with 2.5 years of service submits technical resignation to join another Central Government post. Is he eligible for Transfer Travelling Allowance (TTA)?

(a) No — minimum 3 years of service is mandatory for TTA
(b) Yes — confirmed employees are entitled to TTA regardless of years of service
(c) Yes — but only 50% of TTA is admissible for service less than 3 years
(d) Cannot be determined from these facts without reconciling the 2015 and 2022 provisions

Answer key withdrawn for this item — no option is validated here.

Reason: The question assumes a categorical result from confirmation status. The 2015 substituted Rule 4(4) and DoPT O.M. para 3.6.3 (2022) require reconciliation; see the authority note in Chapter 13. The guide does not decide this employee's entitlement.

Temporary StaffQ2. Temporary Employee with 2 Years Service

Question: A temporary Central Government employee with 2 years of regular continuous service is selected for another post through proper channel. On technical resignation, he is entitled to:

(a) Joining time with pay and full TTA
(b) Joining time without pay and no TTA
(c) No joining time and no TTA
(d) No categorical answer; check substituted Rule 4(4) and DoPT O.M. para 3.6.3

Answer key withdrawn for this item — no option is validated here.

Reason: DoPT O.M. para 3.6.3 states the three-year restriction, but the 2015 substituted Rule 4(4) may be relevant for a qualifying competitive appointment. The interaction is unresolved in this guide; no individual entitlement conclusion is made.

DefinitionQ3. Conditions for Technical Resignation

Question: Which of the following is NOT a condition for treating a resignation as "technical resignation"?

(a) The employee applied through proper channel
(b) The employee was selected for a post in the same or another Government Department
(c) The employee must have completed 3 years of service
(d) The resignation was required for administrative reasons to take up the new post

✓ Answer: (c)

Reasoning: Technical-resignation status itself has no stated minimum-service condition in the cited provisions. DoPT O.M. para 3.6.3 states a three-year restriction for temporary employees, while the interaction with substituted Rule 4(4) (2015) for qualifying competitive appointments is unresolved here. The two issues must not be conflated.

Special CaseQ4. Applied Before Joining Government Service

Question: An employee applied for a competitive exam BEFORE joining Government service. On selection, he was required to resign from his current post. Under what conditions can this be treated as technical resignation?

(a) Never — since the application was not through proper channel
(b) Only if he had informed the new department about his current employment
(c) If he intimated the details on joining AND the authority is satisfied that the application would have been forwarded through proper channel had he been in service
(d) Only with special approval from the Secretary of the Department

✓ Answer: (c)

Reasoning: Under DoPT O.M. Para 2.5, this benefit IS available if: (i) the employee intimated the details immediately on joining, (ii) at resignation, specifically requested it be treated as technical, and (iii) the authority is satisfied that had the employee been in service on the application date, it would have been forwarded through proper channel.

Lien RulesQ5. Lien Retention Period

Question: A permanent Government servant appointed to another Central Government department/office or State Government post tenders technical resignation. Under the cited lien rule, the normal reversion/lien period is:

(a) 1 year only
(b) 2 years normally, 3 years in exceptional cases
(c) 5 years
(d) Until retirement

✓ Answer: (b)

Reasoning: Under DoPT O.M. Para 3.1(i): "A permanent Government servant appointed in another Central Government Department/Office/State Government has to resign from his parent department unless he reverts within a period of 2 years, or 3 years in exceptional cases." The exceptional case (Para 3.1(ii)) is when the employee is not confirmed in the new department within 2 years.

DefinitionQ6. Word "Technical" Not Mentioned

Question: A Government servant submits a plain resignation (not mentioning "technical") to join another Government post for which he had applied through proper channel. This resignation:

(a) Will NOT be treated as technical resignation since the word was not used
(b) Will be treated as technical resignation if all conditions are met, regardless of whether the word "technical" was mentioned
(c) Requires a fresh application to convert it to technical resignation
(d) Is valid only if the new department specifically requests it

✓ Answer: (b)

Reasoning: Under DoPT O.M. Para 2.1: "The resignation will be treated as technical resignation if these conditions are met, even if the Government servant has not mentioned the word 'Technical' while submitting his resignation." This is a very commonly tested point.

Core RuleQ7. Benefits NOT Carried Forward

Question: Which benefit is NOT carried forward on technical resignation?

(a) Earned Leave balance
(b) LTC entitlement
(c) Pay protection
(d) Minimum qualifying service for promotion in the old post (if employee later reverts)

✓ Answer: (d)

Reasoning (scope-limited): DoPT O.M. para 2.4 states that, if a Government servant rejoins the substantive parent post, service spent in the other department after technical resignation does not count toward minimum qualifying service for promotion in the higher post there. Among these options, (d) is the intended distinction, but do not say that every other benefit is carried forward on identical terms: LTC has a separate fresh-recruit rule for specified cases under para 2.3, and leave, pay protection, pension/GPF/NPS each follow their own conditions. This item and answer remain unverified as part of the full bank review.

Assertion-ReasonQ8. Assertion-Reason Type

Question:
Assertion (A): A confirmed employee with 2 years of service gets full TTA on technical resignation.
Reason (R): The 3-year service requirement for TTA applies only to temporary employees.

(a) Both A and R are true, and R is the correct explanation of A
(b) Both A and R are true, but R is NOT the correct explanation of A
(c) A is true but R is false
(d) A is false but R is true

Answer key withdrawn — this assertion/reason item is not scored in the updated guide.

Reason: The statements embed a categorical interpretation of the unresolved 2015 / 2022 authority interaction. See Chapter 13; do not infer entitlement from confirmation alone.

State GovtQ9. State Government Employee Eligibility

Question: A State Government employee (temporary) is selected for a Central Government post through proper channel. On joining the Central Government post, is he entitled to joining time and TTA?

(a) Yes — all State Government employees get joining time
(b) No — temporary State Government employees are not entitled to joining time under CCS (Joining Time) Rules, 1979
(c) Yes — if he has 3 years of service
(d) Only joining time, not TTA

Key withdrawn — Q9 is unscored pending source and scope review.

What the checked texts establish: The official G.S.R. 229(E), 27.03.2015, substitutes CCS (Joining Time) Rule 4(4) for qualifying open competitive appointments and lists Central Government employees and permanent/provisionally permanent State Government employees, subject to the stated past-service option. The Gazette's Rule 5(4) amendment concerns distance, not that eligibility test. The 24.11.2022 DoPT consolidation was reviewed in a Government-hosted NESTS reproduction; it addresses joining time and TTA separately in paras 3.6.1 and 3.6.3. The attempted direct DoPT archive route returned HTTP 500. Because this stem asks for both entitlements and the former rationale cited the wrong sub-rule, no combined answer is scored here; check the exact appointment route and all applicable conditions.

Previous key/rationale — retained for audit only, not valid as an answer

Previous key: (b). Previous rationale: Under CCS (Joining Time) Rules, 1979, Rule 5(4): "Central Government employees and permanent/provisionally permanent State Government employees will be entitled to joining time." Temporary State Government employees are specifically NOT covered. This is a critical distinction — State Govt temporary employees, regardless of years of service, do not get joining time or TTA.

LTC RulesQ10. LTC Entitlement on Technical Resignation

Question: A Government servant resigns within 8 years of initial appointment and joins another post in Government after a valid technical resignation. Under the cited DoPT LTC instruction, the employee’s LTC entitlement is regulated as follows:

(a) Fresh LTC block starts from the date of joining the new department
(b) The employee is treated as a fresh recruit for 8 years from the date of initial appointment under Government
(c) LTC is completely forfeited on technical resignation
(d) The employee retains the same LTC block and entitlement without any modification

✓ Answer: (b)

Reasoning: Under DoPT O.M. Para 2.3: "In case of a Government Servant who resigns within 8 years of his appointment and joins another post in the Government after Technical Resignation, the Government Servant will be treated as a fresh recruit for a period of 8 years from the date of his initial appointment under Government." So if he has served 4 years already, he is treated as a fresh recruit for the remaining 4 years of the 8-year fresh recruit period.

Probation CaseQ11. Employee on Probation

Question: An employee on probation (1 year completed, 1 year remaining) is selected for another Central Government post through proper channel. He submits technical resignation. Which statement is correct?

(a) He gets full TTA and joining time pay since he applied through proper channel
(b) He gets joining time (days) but no joining time pay and no TTA
(c) He cannot submit technical resignation during probation
(d) No categorical answer; check substituted Rule 4(4) and DoPT O.M. para 3.6.3

Answer key withdrawn for this item — no option is validated here.

Reason: The scenario may involve substituted Rule 4(4) if it is a qualifying competitive appointment and the rule conditions are met. Read that provision with DoPT O.M. para 3.6.3; the interaction is unresolved in this guide. Technical-resignation status remains a separate question.

ReversionQ12. Reverting to Old Department

Question: A confirmed employee joins a new post on technical resignation. After 6 months, he wants to revert to his old department. Which statement is correct?

(a) He cannot revert — technical resignation is final
(b) He can revert if his lien is still valid (within 2-3 years)
(c) He can revert only with special permission from DoPT
(d) He can revert but loses all service benefits in the new department

✓ Answer: (b)

Reasoning: If he retained lien on the old post (which is valid for 2 years, extendable to 3 in exceptional cases), he can revert. The service in the new department counts for pension, leave, and increment, but NOT for seniority or promotion eligibility in the old department (DoPT O.M. Para 2.4).

Administrative IssueQ13. Department Didn't Forward Application

Question: An employee applied for a post through proper channel but the department delayed forwarding the application. The employee got selected and now wants to submit technical resignation. The department says "we never forwarded your application, so it's not through proper channel." What is correct?

(a) The employee loses all benefits since the department didn't forward the application
(b) The resignation can still be treated as technical if the employee can prove he submitted the application through proper channel
(c) The employee must get special approval from DoPT
(d) The employee should withdraw from the new post

Verification status: UNVERIFIED — do not score; no key assigned.

Reason: The reviewed DoPT technical-resignation text says applications not permitted to be forwarded through proper channel are not treated as technical resignation; the special pre-service-application exception has its own conditions. This stem does not establish whether forwarding was authorized, refused, delayed, or whether the special exception applies. Obtain the current applicable instruction and competent-authority decision; do not assume proof of submission alone resolves the issue.

PSU CaseQ14. Joining a PSU Post

Question: An employee is selected for a PSU post through proper channel. Is this a valid technical resignation?

(a) No — technical resignation is only for Government posts, not PSU
(b) Yes — if the PSU is wholly or substantially owned by the Government
(c) Yes — all PSU posts qualify for technical resignation
(d) No — PSU employees are not Government servants

Verification status: UNVERIFIED — do not score; no key assigned.

Reason: “PSU” is not a sufficiently precise category by itself. The applicable DoPT instruction’s current scope, the entity’s legal status, recruitment route, competent-authority permission, and any service-specific conditions were not verified here. Government ownership alone does not establish every technical-resignation benefit.

Post AbolitionQ15. New Post Abolished After Joining

Question: An employee on technical resignation joins a new post. The new post is abolished after 1 year. What happens?

(a) The employee becomes unemployed
(b) The employee can revert to the old post if lien is still valid
(c) The employee is absorbed in another post in the new department
(d) The employee gets compensation and must find a new job

Verification status: UNVERIFIED — do not score; no key assigned.

Reason: The current applicable lien/technical-resignation rule and the consequences of abolition of the new post were not verified against primary text. A valid lien may be relevant, but it does not by itself establish the categorical accommodation and surplus-staff outcomes stated in the former rationale. Check the specific appointment, lien, abolition and cadre-control orders.

Chapter 17

Frequently Asked Questions (FAQ)

Chapter study mapEDITORIAL FAQ / OPEN
FocusTreat FAQs as prompts for finding the governing rule, not as authority.
Source reviewThese are author-generated FAQs, not official Government FAQs; their legal propositions are not individually source-verified.
Study moveTurn each answer into a question: what source, scope, date and exception would prove it?
Q: I'm on probation (1.5 years completed). Can I apply for another post through proper channel?
Applying during probation and technical-resignation status are separate from TTA / Joining Time Pay entitlement. The 2015 substituted Rule 4(4) and DoPT O.M. para 3.6.3 (2022) require case-specific reconciliation; no categorical yes/no is given here. Lien is a separate issue and depends on the applicable rules and facts.
Q: My confirmation order is pending (probation completed but order not issued). Am I "confirmed" or "temporary"?
The service-status question must be determined under the applicable service rules; do not assume a pending order automatically settles it. In any event, confirmation status alone does not resolve the interaction between substituted Rule 4(4) (2015) and DoPT O.M. para 3.6.3 (2022). Ask the competent authority for a written decision; this guide does not promise full TTA.
Q: I have 2.8 years of service and am confirmed. Will I get TTA?
No categorical answer is given. DoPT O.M. para 3.6.3 and the 2015 substituted Rule 4(4) must be read together, including the latter's conditions. Confirmation alone is not treated here as determinative; request a written decision from the competent authority.
Q: What if my department refuses to forward my application through proper channel?
The department can refuse only on valid grounds (e.g., disciplinary proceedings, bond obligation, shortage of staff). If the refusal is arbitrary, you can appeal to higher authorities. If you apply directly without proper channel and get selected, it will NOT be treated as technical resignation — you'll lose all past service benefits.
Q: I'm a temporary employee with 2.5 years of service. If I get confirmed next month, will I then be eligible for TTA?
Confirmation by itself does not resolve the 2015 Rule 4(4) / 2022 DoPT O.M. interaction. Apply the relevant conditions and seek a written departmental decision; this guide does not decide entitlement.
Q: Does the probation period count towards the 3-year service requirement for temporary employees?
Whether probation counts as regular continuous service depends on the applicable rule and service record. DoPT O.M. para 3.6.3 states a restriction for temporary employees under three years; qualifying competitive appointments may also engage substituted Rule 4(4) (2015). The interaction is unresolved here, so no yes/no entitlement answer is provided.
Q: I'm on deputation to a PSU. Can I apply for another Central Government post through proper channel?
Yes. Your application should be forwarded by your parent department (not the PSU). If selected, you submit technical resignation from the parent department post. TTA is calculated from your deputation station (current place of duty) to the new post.
Q: What happens to my GPF/NPS account on technical resignation?
Your GPF account is transferred to the new office under Rule 35 of GPF (CS) Rules, 1960. Your NPS account (PRAN + PRA balance) is also transferred to the new office. Both continue uninterrupted.
Q: Can I retain lien if I join a State Government post?
Yes. A permanent Central Government employee appointed to a State Government post retains lien on the parent post for 2 years (extendable to 3 in exceptional cases). You must revert within this period or the lien ceases.
Q: What if I don't mention "technical resignation" in my resignation letter?
It doesn't matter. Under DoPT O.M. Para 2.1, if all conditions are met (applied through proper channel, selected for Government post, required to resign), the resignation WILL be treated as technical resignation even if you didn't use the word "technical." However, it's advisable to mention it explicitly to avoid confusion.

Rapid-Revision Recap — Chapter 17: FAQ as a source-finding exercise

  • These are author-generated questions, not official Government FAQs; answers are not individually source-verified.
  • Convert each answer into: “Which current primary source, paragraph, scope, date and exception would prove this?”
  • If the answer depends on the 2015/2022 Joining Time–TTA interaction or an unverified FR/SR/GIO, stop at unresolved rather than guessing.

AO Examination Mastery Module — Travelling Allowance (TA) Rules (Cut-off 22 Nov 2026)

Baseline: SR 17–195 · DoE OM 13.07.2017 (7th CPC) · Level 6–8 Air Allowed in TA · Tour/Transfer Claim 60 days · Retirement Claim 180 days · CTG 80%.

1. Night-Before-Exam TA Cheat Sheet (Critical Figures & Deadlines)

ParameterOfficial Rule / FigureAuthority
Air Travel Entitlement (TA)Level 14+: Business · Level 6–13: Economy (Level 6–8 allowed in TA!)DoE OM 13.07.2017
TA claim-submission windowsTour / Transfer / Training: 60 days whether or not an advance was drawn · Retirement: 180 days under the specific 2021 O.M.GFR 290; DoE O.Ms 13.03.2018 and 15.06.2021
TA Advance AdjustmentInspected 2005 Compendium Rule 51 says 15 days for tour advances; currency unverified. No general 30-day transfer deadline located.Compendium Rules 43, 51; verify current order
Composite Transfer Grant (CTG)80% for eligible transfers at 20 km or more (100% for specified A&N/Lakshadweep transfers); below 20 km/same station, one-third of normal CTG only if residence actually changes, otherwise nil.DoE OM 13.07.2017
Daily Allowance (DA) & Absence<6 hrs: 30% · 6–12 hrs: 70% · >12 hrs: 100% (Food + Hotel + Local Travel)DoE OM 13.07.2017

2. 5-Step TA Audit & Claim Settlement Flowchart

  1. Sanction: Confirm the tour/transfer order issued by the Controlling Officer.
  2. Entitlement: Check Pay Level, mode entitlement and (if air) the applicable booking channel.
  3. DA and hotel: Apply the absence band (30%/70%/100%) and check hotel, food and local-travel documentation separately.
  4. Transfer/retirement: Check CTG conditions and personal-effects weight/rate separately.
  5. Settlement: Apply the 60-day / 180-day claim-submission window; verify any advance-adjustment deadline from a separate current authority.

3. Common TA Audit Objections & How to Reply

  • Objection: Level 7 officer flew on official tour. Reply: Valid! DoE OM 13.07.2017 entitles Level 6–8 to Economy Air on TA (restriction is only on LTC).
  • Objection: Transfer TA submitted after 75 days. Reply: It is outside the ordinary 60-day window. Do not promise routine HOD/FA condonation; check the applicable DoE O.M. and any valid specific relaxation. The 13.03.2018 O.M. para 5 addresses claims kept in abeyance for more than one year after HOD investigation; it is not a general late-claim extension.
  • Objection: Same-station transfer CTG claimed at 80%. Reply: Restricted to 1/3rd of CTG only if actual change of residence is proved.

4. TA Numerical & File-Noting Lab

Numerical: Basic Pay ₹60,000, Transfer >20 km (mainland) → CTG = 80% × ₹60,000 = ₹48,000. If same-station with residence change → 1/3rd × ₹48,000 = ₹16,000.
Model Note: "Shri [Name], Level 7, performed official tour from [Date] to [Date] (absence 14 hrs). Claim submitted within 60 days with hotel/food vouchers. Passed for ₹[Amount] under DoE OM 13.07.2017."

4. Which rule applies? Claim-classification decision tree

Follow the branches in order. Classify the purpose first; mode of transport, advance status, and pay level come later.

  1. Is this travel for official duty or a personal LTC journey?
    • Official duty, transfer, retirement, training, or a special family case: go to Step 2 and use the applicable FR/SR/DoE TA order.
    • Personal leave travel under LTC: go to Step 3 and use the CCS (LTC) Rules and separate DoPT LTC orders. Do not treat LTC as official-tour TA.
    • Defence / CAPF / another separately governed service: stop and identify the current service-specific/PCDA authority; this civilian guide may not govern.
  2. If official TA, which subcategory?
    • Tour / transfer / ordinary training: identify the current DoE order, pay/mode entitlement, journey and filing trigger. Ordinary claim submission is 60 days whether or not an advance was drawn. Check advance adjustment separately. If air travel is claimed, apply the ATA instruction within its air-ticket scope; rail booking follows the relevant rail rule.
    • Retirement TA: use the separate 180-day order and its conditions; do not use the ordinary 60-day line.
    • Training TA/DA: identify the actual course sanction and facility terms. Current SR 164/GIO schedules and station-sequence claims remain pending [D]; DoE O.M. 01.02.2018 separately addresses free boarding/lodging. Do not apply a universal reset or 30/150 formula.
    • Death-in-service family TA: use the SR 148 family-specific eligibility, advance, journey and adjustment provisions; do not import the ordinary tour-advance clock.
    • Technical resignation / Joining Time / TTA after appointment: compare G.S.R. 229(E) dated 27.03.2015 with DoPT O.M. dated 24.11.2022, para 3.6.3. The interaction remains unresolved here: get a written decision; do not infer entitlement from resignation status alone.
  3. If LTC, what is the mode and advance status?
    • Rail: use the CCS (LTC) rail entitlement and booking rules. The air ATA requirement is not a blanket rule for all rail tickets; eligible LTC e-ticketing through Indian Railways is addressed separately in the DoPT FAQ.
    • Air: check the employee’s LTC air entitlement and the applicable special dispensation. Use the prescribed ATA channel under the cited LTC-air orders. A 21-day lead is encouragement; the 30-day advance-request instruction is only for employees entitled to air travel.
    • Claim after return: determine whether an LTC advance was drawn: normally one month with advance / three months without, subject to the limited FAQ-described relaxation. Do not convert these to fixed day counts.
  4. Separate the four clocks: (1) claim submission, (2) advance adjustment, (3) request to draw an advance, and (4) recommended booking lead time. Write the event, source paragraph, responsible authority, and date for each. If a current signed authority cannot be located, mark the point UNRESOLVED and ask the competent office in writing.
Decision boundary: This tree directs the reader to the rule set; it does not decide an individual’s entitlement or replace sanction, audit, or a competent-authority ruling.

5. Practical claim-preparation checklist

Use this as a file-completeness aid—not as a guarantee that a claim is admissible.

Before travel or transfer

  • □ Classify the journey first: official tour, transfer, retirement, training, LTC, or another category. Do not import an LTC condition into a TA claim (or vice versa).
  • □ Locate the current primary order for the category and record its number, date, paragraph, effective date, and any later amendment.
  • □ Keep written sanction/approval, duty dates, destination, route, purpose, pay level, and the authority approving the journey.
  • □ Check the applicable mode/class and booking instructions for this journey type. Do not rely on the unverified July 2026 LTC advisory.
  • □ If an advance is requested, obtain the governing current ceiling, adjustment trigger/date, and recovery terms from the competent office in writing. Do not assume a general 90% cap or a general 30-day transfer deadline.

When preparing the claim

  • □ Separate fare, Daily Allowance, hotel, local conveyance, CTG, baggage, and conveyance claims; apply each component’s own conditions and documentary requirements.
  • □ Keep tickets/boarding passes, receipts or certificates where required, route evidence, sanction, dates of departure/return, and proof of any permitted exception.
  • □ Use the ordinary 60-day or retirement 180-day claim window only for the relevant category and from the correct trigger date. Do not treat either as an advance-adjustment deadline.
  • □ For transfer claims, document station/residence changes and the basis for CTG, family travel, baggage, and vehicle transport; check current limits and ownership requirements.
  • □ If technical resignation is involved, decide that status separately from TTA and Joining Time Pay. For the 2015/2022 interaction, attach both authorities and request a written ruling.
  • □ Keep a copy of the submitted claim, acknowledgement, calculation sheet, sanctions, and any clarification or relaxation.
Stop-and-check cases: missing primary authority; a deadline inferred from an old compendium; a claim relying on the unlocated LTC advisory; or a technical-resignation case where Rule 4(4) / para 3.6.3 could affect TTA or Joining Time Pay. Escalate rather than guessing.

6. Worked calculations—with assumptions stated

Example A — double Transport Allowance arithmetic (not an eligibility ruling). Assume the applicable higher-TPTA-city base amount is ₹7,200 per month, the employee meets the separate disability-related double-allowance conditions, and DA is 63% for the relevant period. Calculation before payroll rounding: ₹7,200 × 2 × (1 + 0.63) = ₹23,472 per month. The regular-rate arithmetic is ₹7,200 × 1.63 = ₹11,736. Confirm the employee category, city, base rate, effective date, and payroll rounding under the current order.

Example B — CTG arithmetic (conditions must be checked). If the normal CTG rate is 80% and basic pay is ₹60,000, then ₹60,000 × 0.80 = ₹48,000. If the applicable same-station / residence-change rule limits CTG to one-third of the normal amount, then ₹48,000 ÷ 3 = ₹16,000. This is arithmetic only: verify the transfer category, distance, actual residence change, and governing order before treating either amount as payable.

Example C — Daily Allowance percentage arithmetic. Using the guide’s displayed Level 9–11 Food DA ceiling of ₹1,125, a valid 6–12-hour tour absence at 70% gives ₹1,125 × 0.70 = ₹787.50. If a distinct local-journey rule applies a 50% factor to that component, the arithmetic is ₹1,125 × 0.70 × 0.50 = ₹393.75. Confirm the journey category, time band, applicable table, and rounding; do not combine these factors unless the rule applies.

7. Glossary & common confusions

TermMeaning / caution
TATravelling Allowance; a family of entitlements, not one flat payment.
DAAmbiguous abbreviation: Dearness Allowance when linked to pay, versus Daily Allowance for duty travel. Spell it out when the meaning could be unclear.
TTATransfer Travelling Allowance. Technical-resignation status alone does not settle TTA entitlement.
CTGComposite Transfer Grant; distinguish its percentage and conditions from fare, baggage, and other transfer components.
LTCLeave Travel Concession; it is not official-tour TA, and its booking, vehicle, and claim rules may differ.
Claim-submission limitTime to submit a claim after the journey, under the applicable rule.
Advance adjustmentSeparate accounting/settlement obligation after an advance. Do not infer it from the claim-submission limit.
Technical resignationA status under applicable DoPT conditions; it is analytically separate from Joining Time Pay and TTA entitlement.
Primary vs secondary sourceA signed Government rule/order is primary authority. A handbook, compilation, or summary is a research aid unless its legal status and currency are established.
